*Canon of the Cross, Ode 9. Heirmos:* The Son of the beginningless Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us, to enlighten those in darkness, to gather together those that were scattered; therefore the all-hymned Theotokos do we magnify.[^1699]

*Troparion 1:* Heal my affliction and misery, O unfathomable Word, by Thy wound, and cleanse by Thy Passion, O Lord, God of my salvation, the image that was buried by crafty passions.[^1700]

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast seen lifted up upon the cypress, the pine, and the cedar, O Master, by reason of Thy goodness, being One of the Holy Trinity, having one hypostasis in two natures, that Thou mightest save mankind.[^1701]

*Martyrikon 1:* Clothed with the Cross as with a shield, O martyrs, ye appeared unharmed by all the arrows of the author of evil; therefore do ye now tread him down, ever mocking him as a feeble bird.[^1702]

*Martyrikon 2:* The earth indeed, opening its mouth, received your blood, and heaven received your divine spirits; and with the fiery ranks ye stand before the divine throne, O trophy-bearing martyrs, unshakable pillars of the Church.[^1703]

*Theotokion:* Thou didst renew the corrupted nature of the forefather, having given birth beyond nature unto the Creator of all nature, yet remaining a virgin; whom beholding of old hanging upon the Cross, thou didst exclaim, O all-pure Virgin Mother.[^1704]

*Another Heirmos:* From God the Father, God the Word, who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, *On Wednesday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Another Heirmos (continued):* ineffably incarnate of the holy Virgin for our sake: we the faithful with one accord magnify in hymns.[^1705]

*Troparion 1:* Count me worthy of divine love for mankind, O Maiden, who alone didst ineffably give birth unto the God who loves mankind, who borrowed flesh from thee; and deliver me from future flame and every torment, who glorify thee with love. *(Twice)*

*Troparion 2:* Having acquired thee alone as a steadfast protectress, as hope and wall and expectation, as a firm shelter, an invincible confirmation, and an unwavering haven, a strong refuge: we are all saved, O All-Hymned One.[^1706]

*Troparion 3:* O Maiden who didst give birth unto the divine Light: enlighten my heart which is darkened by the many assaults of passions and thoughts of the stranger, ever granting unto me a drop, O Virgin, that cleanses me from the defilements of sin.[^1707]

*It is truly meet: and a bow. Litany, Photogogicon, and the customary psalms.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 2.*
*Sticheron 1:* The wood of Thy Cross, O Christ God, Thou hast shown forth as a tree of life unto us that believe in Thee, and thereby Thou didst abolish the power of him that held the dominion of death, and didst quicken us who were slain by sin; therefore do we cry aloud unto Thee: O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee![^1708]

*Verse:* We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord... And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their sons.[^1709]

*Sticheron 2:* Voluntarily becoming poor for the sake of the poverty of Adam, O Christ God, Thou camest upon earth, taking flesh from the Virgin, and didst accept crucifixion, that Thou mightest free us from the bondage of the enemy. Lord, glory to Thee![^1710]

*Verse:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and direct the works of our hands upon us; yea, direct Thou the work of our hands.[^1711]

*Sticheron 3:* Every city and country honors your relics, O passion-bearing martyrs! For having lawfully suffered, ye received crowns from heaven; and therefore are ye the boast of hierarchs, the victory of kings, and the beauty of churches.[^1712]

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
*Stavrotheotokion:* When thou didst behold hanging upon the Tree the all-ripe Cluster, O Pure One, whom thou didst bear without cultivation in thy womb, *On Wednesday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Stavrotheotokion (continued):* weeping, thou didst exclaim and cry aloud: O Child! Drop down sweetness, whereby all drunkenness of passions shall be taken away, through the kindness of me that bare Thee, being entreated by Thine own tender compassion![^1713]

*Then: It is good: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparion, and litany; then the First Hour, and the dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy:*
*Beatitudes, Tone 2:*
*Strophe 1:* We bring unto Thee the voice of the thief, and we pray: Remember us, O Savior, in Thy kingdom.[^1714]

*Strophe 2:* When Thou wast lifted up upon the Cross, O Savior, Thou didst raise up together with Thyself the whole of human nature, which unceasingly hymns Thee.[^1715]

*Strophe 3:* With Thy spear Thou didst tear asunder the handwriting of Adam, having inscribed him in the book of the living, O Lover of mankind.[^1716]

*Strophe 4:* Having become imitators of Him who endured crucifixion, ye were shown forth as partakers of His glory also, O martyrs.[^1717]

*Glory... Trinity:*
*Trinitarion:* Let the transcendent and beginningless Father, and the co-beginningless Son, be hymned in one honor and glory together with the Holy Spirit.[^1718]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Theotokion:* When thou didst behold upon the Cross Him whom thou hadst brought forth without seed, weeping, O Maiden, thou didst hymn His long-suffering.[^1719]

## Thursday: The Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas[^1720]

*On Wednesday Evening.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Holy Apostles, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
*Sticheron 1:* Ye watered all the earth, even as spiritual rivers parting from Eden, which having plowed, ye sowed the saving preaching, and reaped a fruitful ear of corn, O wise ones, even the souls of the saved, hiding these in noetic storehouses, O disciples of the Lord, as a multi-precious treasure.[^1721]

*Sticheron 2:* From the darkness of passions and pleasures deliver my multi-passioned heart, ye who are luminaries of the noetic Orient, and most radiant enlighteners, proclaiming unto all the Sun who destroyed the deception of the night of evil faith; whom do ye entreat, as eyewitnesses of Him, that He may also enlighten our minds.[^1722]

*On Wednesday Evening, Tone 2.*

*Sticheron 3:* Truly as tablets of the new grace inscribed by God, as initiated ministers and animate scrolls were ye revealed, bearing the saving Word written by the Spirit, by the Father's finger; therefore did ye pass through all the ends of the earth, clearly showing forth unto all men the Orthodox faith that leads unto the path of heaven.[^1723]

*Other Stichera, to the Holy Wonderworker Nicholas, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
*Sticheron 1:* Having dwelt sensibly in Myra, thou wast truly revealed as myrrh, having been anointed with noetic myrrh, O holy Nicholas, hierarch of Christ! Thou sheddest abroad the immortal fragrance of sweet savor upon them that with faith flee beneath thy shelter, delivering them from assaults and tribulations and sorrows, O Father, by thy prayers unto the Lord.[^1724]

*Sticheron 2:* Held fast by a multitude of temptations, beset by the surging waves of life, drowning in the billows of distress, and encompassed by sorrows, upon thee do I place all my hope, O Father Nicholas! Grant me deliverance from all cruel evils by thy prayers, O blessed one, which thou offerest unto our Master and God.[^1725]

*Sticheron 3:* Overtake and illuminate me with the light of grace, who am encompassed by the darkness and gloom of thoughts, surrounded by demonic deceits, tempest-tossed by carnal passions, and defeated by the law of sin; for thou art a light in the world, illuminated by divine splendor, O Nicholas worthy of all blessedness.[^1726]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion, Tone 2:*
*Theotokion:* The Word who is equal in honor to the Father and the Holy Spirit, like a great Sun that shone forth upon earth in the latter times from the Virgin Maiden of God, sent you forth like rays, O glorious Apostles, who enlighten all men in the darkness of delusion with the light of faith, and lead them unto it by divine teachings.[^1727]

*Then: O Gladsome Light: Prokeimenon of the day.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Apostles, Tone 2.*
*Sticheron 1:* Thou hast magnified throughout the universe, O Savior, the names of the pre-eminent Apostles; for they learned heavenly things, and gave ineffable healings unto the earthborn; and their shadows alone cured illnesses: they who from fishermen worked wonders, and who from Jews taught the theology of the grace of teachings; for whose sake, O Merciful One, grant us great mercy.[^1728]

*Verse:* Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... Even so our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have mercy on us.[^1729]

*Sticheron 2:* Ever reproached because of our unjust deeds, yet fleeing unto Thee who art truly God, *On Wednesday Evening, Tone 2.*

*Sticheron 2 (continued):* we bring unto Thee the voice of Thy disciples, saying: Save us, O Master, we perish! Show now also unto our enemies, we pray, how Thou protectest Thy people, and savest from perils by the prayers of the Apostles, overlooking sins because of Thy great goodness. Lord, glory to Thee![^1730]

*Verse:* Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... for we are greatly filled with contempt. Our soul is exceedingly filled with the reproach of the prosperous, and with the contempt of the proud.[^1731]

*Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon):* Great is the glory which ye acquired through faith, O holy ones! For not only did ye overcome the enemy in your sufferings, but even after death ye drive away spirits and heal the sick, O physicians of souls and bodies! Pray unto the Lord, that our souls may be saved.[^1732]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion, Tone 2:*
*Theotokion:* As a fruitful olive tree, the Virgin grew Thee forth, the living Fruit, to bear fruit for the world, even great and rich mercy.[^1733]

*Then: Now lettest Thou: Trisagion; and after Our Father: troparion and dismissal.*

*On Wednesday at Compline:*
*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought forth from the bondage of Egypt; for He hath been glorified.[^1734]

*Refrain:* Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.[^1735]

*Troparion 1:* Him who with soul and lips piously confesses thee to be the true Theotokos, O Pure One, deliver now from cruel perils, illnesses, and transgressions, O All-Immaculate One.

*Troparion 2:* We know the whole abyss of graces that abides within thee; therefore, having recourse earnestly unto thy divine protection, O Theotokos, we are saved.

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* On behalf of us that hymn thee, O All-Pure One: pray unto Him who was incarnate of thy most pure and honorable blood, that we may be delivered from transgressions and bitter diseases.

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* As our boast have all we the faithful acquired thee: a refuge and confirmation and rejoicing, and salvation for our souls, our hope and wall, O Lady full of the grace of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish us in Thee, O Lord, who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us that hymn Thee.[^1736]

*Troparion 1:* By thy prayers, O Pure One, grant that God, whom thou didst bear, may be *On Wednesday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 1 (continued):* easily propitiated toward thy servants who flee unto thy protection and faithfully venerate thy childbirth.[^1737]

*Troparion 2:* Hear the words of my prayer from the depth of my heart, O Virgin, which I offer unto thee, O All-Hymned One, and save me from passions and assaults.

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Direct all my life, O Virgin, my hope and protectress, delivering me from the assaults of temptations and dire troubles, O Bride of God.

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* As thou didst carry in thine arms the hypostatic Wisdom of God, O Mother of God: pray that we may be delivered from ignorance and delusion.[^1738]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thine incomprehensible power, O Lover of mankind.[^1739]

*Troparion 1:* For the wounds of my soul and the defilements of the flesh, grant healing, O Sovereign Lady who didst give birth unto God.

*Troparion 2:* Strengthen me by hope and faith, O Virgin, who am defiled by passions, thoughts, and the surging billows of life.

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* From assault, and tempest, and perils deliver me by thy prayers, O Mother of God, who alone art all-hymned.

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Snatch me away, O Virgin, who am tossed by the billows of life, guiding me unto thy haven.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord: guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for we know no other God save Thee.[^1740]

*Troparion 1:* Having an invincible weapon against the various temptations of the enemy, we who purely know thee to be the Theotokos are manifestly delivered from every affliction.

*Troparion 2:* Higher than the Cherubim, thou didst give birth unto the fulfillment of the Law, the Word of God, the incarnate only-begotten Son; entreat Him on behalf of thy servants.[^1741]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Bearing in thine arms the Creator of all things, O Pure One: by thy prayers make Him easily propitiated toward us who now flee unto thee with our whole heart.

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* From distress and sickness of soul I bring supplication unto thee, O *On Wednesday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 4 (continued):* wretched one that I am; O thou who alone didst give birth unto the Word, the Author of bounties: take compassion on me and save me.[^1742]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy loving-kindness: Lead me up from corruption, O God.[^1743]

*Troparion 1:* Knowing thee to be the haven of salvation, as I sail upon the sea of sorrowful life, I call upon thee, O Sovereign Lady: Be the pilot of my soul.

*Troparion 2:* I have been stripped of the garment of my chastity, having been afflicted; but O Mother and Ever-Virgin who didst give birth unto God, grant unto me the garment of joy.[^1744]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* I have fallen away from a pure life, having lived in passionate slothfulness; but raise me up, O blessed Sovereign Lady, uniting me unto the commandments of thy Son.

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Count me worthy of thy mercy, O Theotokos, who didst bring forth the all-merciful Word, who by His own blood delivered mankind from corruption.[^1745]

*Then: Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory... both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "We have become partakers of the divine" (Бж҃твеннагѡ бы́хомъ):*
*Sessional Hymn:* Count me worthy of the divine entrance, O Pure One, by thy prayers, O Ever-Virgin; and having broken the bonds of cruel passions, free me from the coming flame.[^1746]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* When the golden image was worshipped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the ungodly decree; cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1747]

*Troparion 1:* Being nailed unto the Cross, He who was incarnate of thee, O Birthgiver of God, tore asunder the handwriting of Adam; Him do thou now entreat, O Virgin, that they may be delivered from all malice who cry in faith: Blessed art thou who didst bear God in the flesh.[^1748]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 2:* I was seized by the cruel darkness of life, and found none to sorrow or suffer with me; by thy brightness, O Virgin, dispel the darkness of transgressions, and illuminate me to sing unto thee: Blessed art thou who didst bear God in the flesh.[^1749]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 3:* Having clothed myself through baptism in the fair garment of the saving commandments, I wretchedly blackened it through slothfulness; but now I flee unto thee for the garment of salvation, asking that through thee I may be clothed once again.[^1750]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Unto Him who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and the flame...[^1751]

*On Wednesday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos (continued):* ...turned into dew: hymn God as Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.[^1752]

*Troparion 1:* Now mine intellect fails, having fallen into the depth of dishonor, for on every side I am encompassed by diverse evils; but do thou, O Virgin, heal me, clothing me with the light of dispassion.[^1753]

*Troparion 2:* Having acquired thee by faith as a firm tower of strength, a step, a guardian, and an intercessor, we are saved, now hymning thy Childbearing, O Most Pure One, and supreme exalting Him unto all ages.[^1754]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* We know thee, O Theotokos, as the dawn of immortality and the wellspring, for thou didst bear the Word of the immortal Father, who delivers from death all them that supreme exalt Him unto all ages.[^1755]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Thou dost ever pour forth streams of healings unto us the faithful, O Pure One; receiving their ungrudging grace, we hymn thy Childbearing, O Pure One, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.[^1756]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* God the Word of God, who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who by eating had grievously fallen into corruption, and who was ineffably incarnate for our sake of the holy Virgin: Him do we the faithful with one mind magnify in songs.[^1757]

*Troparion 1:* O Maiden blessed of God, all my hope have I fervently placed upon thee: save me, O Mother of the true Life, and pray, O Pure One, that I who magnify thee in songs with faith and love may be filled with the perpetual food.[^1758]

*Troparion 2:* Having been revealed, O Virgin, as the portal of divine light, illuminate the mist of my soul with thine immaterial light and splendors, and by thine intercessions count me worthy, O Pure One, to be delivered from eternal fire, that I may unceasingly magnify thee.[^1759]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Beholding us sick in soul and body, and cast into grievous passions, heal us by thy tender compassion, O Sovereign Lady, and release us who are now oppressed by griefs, that with faith and love we may magnify thee in songs.[^1760]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Having dwelt within thy womb, the Son whom the pre-eternal Father begat from the womb became perfect man; and He hath shown thee forth unto us, O Mother of God, as a fountain of graces, who faithfully worship thine ineffable Childbearing.[^1761]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Then: It is truly meet; Trisagion Prayers; and after Our Father: the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*[^1762]

*On Thursday at Matins.*[^1763]
*After the First Stichology, Sessional Hymns of the Apostles, Tone 2.*

*Sessional Hymn 1:* O Christ God, who by Thine ineffable love for mankind didst make fishermen wiser than rhetors, and didst send them forth as preachers throughout all the earth: for their sake establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O Thou who alone dost rest in the saints.[^1764]

*Verse: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*[^1765]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* Being disciples and eyewitnesses of the Wisdom of God, the divine Apostles made foolish the foolish wisdom of the wise and of rhetors, having made the nations wise through the simplicity of their preaching, to hymn orthodoxly the one Creator and Lord.[^1766]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* We magnify thee, O Theotokos, crying aloud: Rejoice, O cloud of the unsetting Light, for thou didst bear within thy bosom the Lord of glory.[^1767]

*After the Second Stichology, Sessional Hymns, Tone 2.*

*Sessional Hymn 1:* Having caught the nations with the net of the Spirit, the fishers taught the ends of the earth to worship Thee together with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God; for their sake establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O sole Merciful One and Lover of mankind.[^1768]

*Verse: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.*[^1769]
*And say again the same Sessional Hymn.*
*After this, Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^1770]

*For the Martyrs:* O passion-bearers of the Lord: blessed is the earth that drank in your blood, and holy are the temples that received your bodies; for at the judgment seat ye confounded the enemy, and proclaimed Christ with boldness! Entreat Him as good, we pray, that our souls may be saved.[^1771]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* We have become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Theotokos and Ever-Virgin, for thou didst bear God incarnate for us; therefore as is meet do we all piously magnify thee.[^1772]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*After the Third Stichology, Sessional Hymns, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Милосе́рдїя сꙋ́щи):*[^1773]
*Sessional Hymn 1:* Having sent forth Thine holy Disciples into the world as radiant luminaries, O Word, Thou didst enlighten all under the sun, delivering all men from the darkness of ignorance. Being ever entreated by their prayers, enlighten my soul that is in darkness, O Lover of mankind, and save me.[^1774]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* With the rays of wonders, O Nicholas, thou dost enlighten all under the sun, dispelling the gloom of sorrows and driving away the assault of perils, being a most fervent intercessor.[^1775]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* O unwedded, all-pure, holy Theotokos and Bride unwedded: together with the Apostles, ever entreat thy Son, whom beyond nature thou didst bear in the flesh, to grant forgiveness of sins, amendment of life, and deliverance from passions unto all who hymn thee.[^1776]

*Canon to the Holy Apostles.*[^1777]
*The work of Theophanes.*
*Ode 1, Tone 2. Heirmos:* In ancient times, the overly-armed power laid low the whole host of Pharaoh in the deep; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the all-glorified Lord: for gloriously hath He been glorified.[^1778]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Having become lightning-flashes of the divine Light through faith, O light-bearing Apostles of the Savior: enlighten me wholly, who am darkened by the blackness of pleasures and have passed all my life in slothfulness. *(Twice)*[^1779]

*Troparion 2:* O disciples and friends of Christ: deliver me from the enemy, whose friend I have become through the adoption of evil things, moving my soul toward the love of Him who loved the human race for the sake of goodness.[^1780]

*Troparion 3:* O my humble soul: before death hasten to repent, and mourn thyself that art deadened, that like four-days-dead Lazarus He may also resurrect thee, as He is merciful, through the prayers of the Apostles.[^1781]

*Theotokion:* Having given birth unto the Good God who works good unto all held fast by corruption, O Most Pure One: entreat Him, O All-Spotless One, together with the Prophets, and the Martyrs, and the Apostles, to deliver all from tribulations.[^1782]

*Another Canon, to the Holy and Great Wonderworker Nicholas.*[^1783]
*(Whose acrostic is: Accept a loving prayer, O Nicholas.) The work of Joseph.*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, who divided the people [sea], whom He brought forth out of Egyptian bondage: for He hath been glorified.[^1784]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Canon to Saint Nicholas, Ode 1 (continued).*
*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Ever standing before the divine throne of grace, O Nicholas: pray that grace and mercy may be granted unto thy servants who call upon thee in faith.[^1785]

*Troparion 2:* God hath shown thee upon earth as a great intercessor for them that are in distress; therefore stand before me by night and by day, preserving me from the snares of the adversary.[^1786]

*Troparion 3:* Knowing thee as a daystar, may I be delivered by thy light-bearing rays from the darkness of temptations, and perils, and every sin, O Father Nicholas.[^1787]

*Theotokion:* Deliver me from bodily sicknesses, and heal the unfathomed wounds of my soul, and snatch me from eternal fire, O sole One graced of God.[^1788]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* The wilderness, the barren church of the nations, hath blossomed like a lily by Thy coming, O Lord, wherein my heart is established.[^1789]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Having acquired the Father's Wisdom by the Spirit as your instructress, O holy ones: ye made foolish the Hellenic wisdom, O all-marvelous eyewitnesses of God. *(Twice)*[^1790]

*Troparion 2:* Loose the barrenness of my soul, ye all-praised ones, and cause it to bring forth a fair fruitfulness of deeds in virtues, as ye are eyewitnesses of the Word worthy of all blessedness.[^1791]

*Troparion 3:* Grievously wounded as I now am by the venomous bite of the enemy, heal me by the supplications of Thine Apostles, O all-merciful Benefactor of all.[^1792]

*Theotokion:* Beseech the all-good God, O Most Pure One, together with all the Apostles, that they who honor thee may be delivered from all harm, and adversity, and perils.[^1793]

*Another [Canon], Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish us in Thee, O Lord, who by the Tree didst slay sin, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.[^1794]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Being the Fountain of healings, O Savior: cure the passions of my soul, and preserve my life, keeping me Thy servant unharmed.[^1795]

*Troparion 2:* Correcting the defeat of my mind, save me from the harm of visible and invisible enemies that war against me, as thou art my great intercessor, O Nicholas.[^1796]

*Troparion 3:* He who alone is Good gave thee unto men as a good protector...[^1797]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 3 (continued):* ...O blessed one; therefore do I pray thee: deliver me from all evils.[^1798]

*Theotokion:* My strength, and joy, and gladness, and a firm wall and protectress art thou, O All-Spotless One, delivering me from temptations and perils.[^1799]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou didst come from the Virgin, not an ambassador, nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and didst save me, the whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1800]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Hungering ever, and wasting away with famine: fill me with the food of salvation, O sole Lover of mankind, by the divine supplications of the Apostles who proclaimed Thee in all the world. *(Twice)*[^1801]

*Troparion 2:* Upon the sea of this world Thou didst bring Thine Apostles as horses of all men, O Lover of mankind, troubling the salty, bitter waters of its unbelief.[^1802]

*Troparion 3:* Having announced Christ the Sun unto them that were in darkness: enlighten me who lie in sinful darkness, O all-praised Apostles, restraining the wicked thoughts of my heart.[^1803]

*Theotokion:* O all-hymned one, who didst give birth unto the all-hymned God: together with the Apostles entreat Him on behalf of them that hymn thee, that we may be delivered from sins, and perils, and affliction.[^1804]

*Another [Canon], Ode 4. Heirmos:* I sing unto Thee, for I heard the report of Thee, O Lord, and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me that was lost; therefore do I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Many-Merciful One.[^1805]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Having adorned the throne with virtues, O Nicholas, thou wast revealed as an honorable ornament unto hierarchs; therefore I pray thee: adorn the uncomeliness of my soul, and save me from worldly snares.[^1806]

*Troparion 2:* Smooth for me the path that leads thither, O all-blessed one; ease me from the billows of life, and lead me up unto the haven of life, O Nicholas, who am enriched with thee as a great intercessor.[^1807]

*Troparion 3:* O hearer of divine words, hearing my words: deliver me from demonic snares, and lawless men, and all approaching adversities, O great Nicholas.[^1808]

*Theotokion:* O holy Theotokos and Sovereign Lady: sanctify me by night and by day, and preserve me, and guide me unto salvation, who fall through many sins and am cast down by demonic assaults...[^1809]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou wast a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have been brought from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Source of Light.[^1810]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* The Great Shepherd sent forth His divine disciples amidst wolves as sheep, transforming them by the divine power of baptism and the beauty of their words. *(Twice)*[^1811]

*Troparion 2:* With divine light ye enlightened hearts that were in the darkness of delusion, O Apostles; therefore I pray you, O all-blessed of God: enlighten me who am darkened by gloomy pleasures.[^1812]

*Troparion 3:* O wretched soul: before the end hasten and repent, crying unto the Lord: I have sinned against Thee, O Master; pardon me for the sake of the Apostles, and save me as Thou art merciful.[^1813]

*Theotokion:* With thy light enlighten me that lie in darkness, O dwelling-place of Light; and together with the Apostles pray, O All-Spotless One, to deliver me from every distress by thy supplications.[^1814]

*Another [Canon], Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord: guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for we know no other God save Thee.[^1815]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* O fulfiller of the Law of God: entreat the good God that I may keep the divine laws, and deliver me from lawless enemies and from demonic harm, O all-blessed Nicholas.[^1816]

*Troparion 2:* Even as in ancient times thou didst appear, O holy one, delivering the three youths: so also deliver me from every sin by thy prayers unto God, O godly-wise Nicholas.[^1817]

*Troparion 3:* O sacred minister of Christ, surety of sinners, great wonderworker: entreat God the Bestower of grace, that He put me not to shame in the hour of judgment.[^1818]

*Theotokion:* O pure Mother of the Lord: standing before me, as thou art good, deliver me who am possessed by many passions from them, that being saved I may hymn thee with soul and heart and tongue.[^1819]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy loving-kindness: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^1820]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Bearing the Living Water: give drink, I pray, unto my soul that melts away with the heat of sin, O Disciples of the Savior. *(Twice)*[^1821] *On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 2:* Having become spiritual heavens, O divinely radiant Apostles, ye declared the ineffable glory of God; pray that we all may attain unto it.[^1822]

*Troparion 3:* Tossed about by waves in the grievous deep, I come unto Thee, O Christ, the Helmsman of all: for the sake of Thine Apostles, direct me unto the haven of salvation.[^1823]

*Theotokion:* Together with all the heavenly Powers, with the Prophets, and Apostles, and Martyrs, entreat thy Son on our behalf, O Bride of God.[^1824]

*Another [Canon], Ode 6. The same Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy loving-kindness: Lead me up from corruption, O God.[^1825]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Having been primate of the people of Myra, O Nicholas, and having perfumed the assemblies of the faithful with thy good deeds: deliver me from the foul stench of transgression.[^1826]

*Troparion 2:* Having acquired a heart brighter than the sun, O Father, enlighten me wholly, driving away the darkness of temptations and sorrows, O Nicholas.[^1827]

*Troparion 3:* Deliver me from every narrow distress, O Nicholas, as thou possessest the broad expanse of mercy, and strengthen me to walk the narrow path unto the Lord.[^1828]

*Theotokion:* At all times I call upon thee, O Most Pure One, that I may find thee a helper, delivering me from all affliction and dreadful torments.[^1829]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The command contrary to God of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread forth spiritual dew unto the pious youths, He that is blessed and all-glorified.[^1830]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Being ignited with the fire of the divine Spirit, O Apostles, ye extinguished the coals of delusion, and kindled the divine desire in the minds of all the faithful; therefore with loud voice we honor you. *(Twice)*[^1831]

*Troparion 2:* Ye hated the world and the things that are in the world; ye loved Christ who in the flesh mingled with men in the world. Entreat Him, O divine Apostles, to set me free from all the cruel things in life.[^1832]

*Troparion 3:* O righteous Judge, Knower of hearts, who alone knowest my secret sins: in the hour of judgment condemn me not, neither send me away into the fire, by the prayers of Thine Apostles.[^1833]

*Theotokion:* Thou wast not consumed by the fire of the Godhead, having ineffably given birth, O Virgin...[^1834]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Theotokion (continued):* ...who knewest not wedlock; therefore together with the Apostles pray, O Pure One, to deliver me from eternal flame who glorify thee.[^1835]

*Another [Canon], Ode 7. Heirmos:* When the golden image was worshipped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the ungodly decree; cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1836]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Every day I am mingled with the fire of temptations, O Father Nicholas; I pass amidst snares like a bird, fleeing beneath thy generous shelter; preserve me unharmed, entreating the good God and Lord.[^1837]

*Troparion 2:* Hearing my words, O Father Nicholas, hasten speedily to come unto my aid, who am tempest-tossed by sorrows, and the necessities of life, and demonic afflictions, that being saved I may hymn thine intercession.[^1838]

*Troparion 3:* Thou who in ancient times didst appear in a dream unto the king, delivering the innocent who were about to die: deliver me from the perils that ever come upon me, and from bodily infirmities, and spiritual sicknesses, O Father Nicholas.[^1839]

*Theotokion:* Thee alone do I have as a helper, O Most Pure One; thee do I inscribe as guardian of my whole life; despise me not, thy servant, O sole protectress of the world, but save me who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^1840]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* In ancient times the fiery furnace in Babylon divided its operation by the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^1841]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Coming upon you bodily in the form of fire, the All-Holy Spirit made you as luminaries, consuming godlessness indeed, but enlightening all that are pious, O divine Apostles of the Word. *(Twice)*[^1842]

*Troparion 2:* Heal my uncorrected heart grievously tempest-tossed by passions, O Compassionate One, I pray: enlighten my soul, and direct my mind that is bent toward malice, through the supplications of Thy disciples.[^1843]

*Troparion 3:* Sigh, O my soul, and weep diligently, before uncomforted weeping overtake thee; and cry aloud unto the Lord: Save me, O Merciful One, through the prayers of Thine Apostles.[^1844]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Theotokion:* In ancient times the furnace that burned not the children prefigured thy Childbearing, O all-spotless Virgin; therefore do I pray thee: together with the Apostles and with all the Prophets, pray that I may be delivered from the fire of Gehenna.[^1845]

*Another [Canon], Ode 8. Heirmos:* Hymn Him as Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages: God, who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew![^1846]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Having received power from God to bind and to loose, O godly-wise Father Nicholas: loose the bonds of my sins by thy prayers, and bind me unto the divine love of the Master who willed to become man.[^1847]

*Troparion 2:* By thy divine visitation visit me by day and by night, smoothing the path for my humble soul, and preserve me, O holy Nicholas, unwounded by the snares of the evil one that assail me.[^1848]

*Troparion 3:* Reach forth a hand unto me for God's help, O Nicholas, and preserve me from the cruel expectation of the enemy, thou who in ancient times didst deliver the youths from bitter death, that I may honor thee as a good protector.[^1849]

*Troparion 4:* When Thou shalt sit upon the dread throne to judge the world, O God: enter not into judgment with Thy servant, but count me worthy of the portion of the saved, through the prayers of Nicholas.[^1850]

*Theotokion:* By thy supernatural childbearing, O Birthgiver of God, thou didst magnify us who were brought low by great, countless evils; therefore do we pray thee, O Most Pure One: magnify in us thy rich mercies.[^1851]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* The Son of the Unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us, to enlighten them that are in darkness and to gather the scattered; therefore do we magnify the all-hymned Theotokos.[^1852]

*Verse: Holy Apostles, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* Return me, O Lord, who am condemned, who am uncorrected, who have disregarded Thy commandments, and have followed demonic delusion unto the harm of my mind, through the prayers of Thine Apostles. *(Twice)*[^1853]

*Troparion 2:* O glorious Apostles, blessed Apostles, and Disciples of the Savior, all-wise preachers: deliver me from all harm, from all wrath, from all sin, from all adversity, and from diverse perils.[^1854]

*Troparion 3:* I bring an uncorrected soul, and a conscience buried together with things of transgression, and a defiled...[^1855]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 3 (continued):* ...heart, and a polluted thought, and I cry unto Thee, O Lover of mankind: for the sake of the Apostles, have compassion on me by Thy mercy.[^1856]

*Theotokion:* The Apostles proclaimed thy Son throughout all the world, O Most Pure One, as God and man; therefore with them entreat Him that in the dread day of judgment they who magnify thee in faith may be delivered from torments.[^1857]

*Another [Canon], Ode 9. Heirmos:* God the Word of God, who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who by eating had grievously fallen into corruption, and who was ineffably incarnate for our sake of the holy Virgin: Him do we the faithful with one mind magnify in songs.[^1858]

*Verse: Holy hierarch Nicholas, pray to God for us.*
*Troparion 1:* A rule of priesthood and a pattern of meekness do I know thee to be, O wise Nicholas! The tempest of passions and perils that rises up against me every day: calm by thy prayers, and preserve me unharmed, O most sacred father.[^1859]

*Troparion 2:* As a most sacred vessel receptive of the divine myrrh mercifully poured forth upon earth: perfume the hearts of us all, O wise one, having been primate of the people of Myra, driving away the stench of temptation by thy prayers.[^1860]

*Troparion 3:* Do thou comfort my soul, O Father, which is invisibly and grievously afflicted by a multitude of evils; do thou calm my measureless temptations which the deceitful one stirs up against me by day and by night, appearing as my good intercessor, O Nicholas.[^1861]

*Troparion 4:* The dread Day of the Master draws nigh at the doors: what wilt thou do, O my soul, having a multitude of sins? Hasten therefore before the end, and cry aloud diligently unto the Lord: Through the prayers of Nicholas Thy hierarch, save me![^1862]

*Theotokion:* Spare me, O Lord, spare me when Thou shalt come to judge, and condemn me not unto the fire, neither rebuke me in Thine anger: the Virgin who bare Thee entreats Thee, O Christ, together with the multitude of the Apostles, and glorious Nicholas.[^1863]

*Then: It is truly meet; and a prostration. Little Litany, Photogogic, and the usual Psalms.*[^1864]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Apostles, Tone 2.*[^1865]
*Sticheron 1:* Thou didst magnify in the universe the names of the supreme Apostles, O Savior; for they learned of heavenly things, and bestowed ineffable healings upon earthly men; and their very shadows cured diseases, working wonders though they were from fishermen, and though from...[^1866]

*On Thursday at Matins, Tone 2.*

...Jews, they theologized the doctrines of grace; for their sake, O Compassionate One, grant unto us great mercy.[^1867]

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced... And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their sons.*[^1868]

*Sticheron 2:* We who are ever assailed by unrighteous deeds and who flee unto Thee, the truly existing God, bring unto Thee the voice of Thy disciples, saying: Master, save us, we perish! Show now also unto our enemies, we beseech Thee, that Thou dost shelter Thy people and deliver them from perils, overlooking our sins through the prayers of the Apostles, according to Thy great goodness: O Lord, glory to Thee![^1869]

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us; and direct Thou the works of our hands upon us; yea, the work of our hands direct Thou.*[^1870]

*For the Martyrs:* The multitude of Thy saints entreat Thee, O Christ: have mercy and save us, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^1871]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* All my hope do I place upon thee, O Mother of God: preserve me under thy shelter.[^1872]

*Then: It is good to give praise; Trisagion Prayers; and after Our Father: the troparia, and litany.*[^1873]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes, Tone 2.*
*Troparion 1:* We bring unto Thee the voice of the thief, and cry aloud unto Thee: Remember us, O Savior, in Thy kingdom.[^1874]

*Troparion 2:* Having traversed the fullness of the world, O Apostles, ye delivered mankind from the darkness of the delusion of impiety, O all-wise ones.[^1875]

*Troparion 3:* By the net of grace, O disciples of the Savior, ye wisely drew up all men from the depth of vanity.[^1876]

*Troparion 4:* Having finished the course and kept the faith, let us the faithful with one accord hymn the passion-bearers of the Lord.[^1877]

*Glory... Triadikon:* Having preached the Son co-equal in operation with the Father together with the Spirit, let us hymn the uncreated Trinity.[^1878]

*Both now... Theotokion:* The boast of the Apostles, the ornament of the sufferers art thou, O Most Pure Virgin, and the salvation of the world.[^1879]

## Friday: The Precious and Life-Giving Cross[^1880]

*On Thursday Evening.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Cross, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

*Sticheron 1:* When Thou, O Savior, wast nailed unto the Cross, the sun seeing it was darkened at fear of Thee, and the veil of the temple was rent in twain, the earth was shaken, and the rocks likewise were split with trembling, unable to endure beholding their Creator and God suffering unjustly on the Tree of Thine own will, and reviled by lawless men.[^1881]

*Sticheron 2:* Utterly cast down unto the earth, utterly wounded, the all-evil serpent lies by a marvelous fall, when Thou, O Lover of mankind, wast lifted up upon the Tree; and Adam who was formerly condemned is loosed from the curse and saved. Therefore we also pray: Save all of us, O Bountiful One, and count us worthy of Thy kingdom.[^1882]

*Sticheron 3:* When the lawless people nailed Thee, the Life of all, unto the Cross, O Savior, then all creation trembled with fear; the kingdoms of hades and the whole dominion of death were cast down by Thy divine power; then also Adam the forefather rejoicing cried out unto Thee: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind![^1883]

*Then the Stichera of the Menaion for the Saint. But if there is no Menaion, we chant these Cross-Theotokia [Stavrotheotokia]:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Гла́съ в҃. Подо́бенъ то́йже):*[^1884]

*Stavrotheotokion 1:* Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Tree of the Cross, O Jesus, the Unwedded Maiden wept and said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who bare Thee? O Inaccessible Light of the Pre-eternal Father! Hasten and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory.[^1885]

*Stavrotheotokion 2:* When the Virgin beheld Life dying upon the Tree, and His side painfully pierced with a spear, weeping she cried aloud: O my Son and my God, what hath the ungrateful assembly repaid Thee? Woe is me! Unable to endure the pangs, I am consumed in my womb, seeing Thee suffering these things, O Master.[^1886]

*Stavrotheotokion 3:* When the Unwedded Maiden saw her Son taken down from the Tree and laid outstretched upon the earth lifeless as a man, embracing Him in her bosom and kissing His lips and eyes, she cried aloud marvelously unto Him: How do I now see Thee, who givest life to all, speechless and motionless? Truly a great wonder![^1887]

*On Thursday Evening, Tone 2.*

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
When the undefiled Ewe-lamb saw her Lamb led voluntarily unto slaughter as a man, weeping she said: Dost Thou now hasten to make me childless, O Christ, who bare Thee? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness surpassing mind and word, O Lover of mankind.[^1888]

*Then: O Gladsome Light; Prokeimenon of the day; Vouchsafe, O Lord.*[^1889]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 2.*
*Sticheron 1:* Save me, O Christ the Savior, by the power of the Cross, Thou Who didst save Peter in the sea, and have mercy on me, O God.[^1890]

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven... So look our eyes unto the Lord our God, until He take pity on us.*[^1891]

*Sticheron 2:* Let Him be crucified! cried out they who continually enjoyed Thy gifts; and the murderers of the righteous asked to receive a malefactor instead of the Benefactor. But Thou wast silent, O Christ, enduring their cruelty, desiring to suffer and to save us, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^1892]

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... Our soul is exceedingly filled: an object of reproach unto the prosperous, and of contempt unto the proud.*[^1893]

*For the Martyrs:* The passion-bearers, having not loved earthly pleasures, were accounted worthy of heavenly good things, and became fellow-citizens with the angels. O Lord, by their prayers, have mercy and save us.[^1894]

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва та̀):*
When the lawless people lifted Thee up upon the Tree, O Savior, the Life of all, then Thy pure and all-spotless Mother standing by and sobbing cried aloud: O my sweet Child! Light of mine eyes! Woe is me! How hast Thou endured to be nailed unto the Cross in the midst of two malefactors, Thou Who didst suspend the earth upon the waters?[^1895]

*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart; Trisagion Prayers; after Our Father: troparion, and the Dismissal.*[^1896]

*On Thursday at Compline.*
*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Of old the transcendent power laid low the entire host of Pharaoh in the deep; and the Word incarnate hath abolished all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord: for He is gloriously glorified.[^1897]

*Refrain: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*
*Troparion 1:* As beautiful, as all-comely, as spotless among wo-...[^1898]

*On Thursday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 1 (continued):* ...-men, God chose thee and took up His abode in thy spotless womb. Entreat Him, O All-Spotless One, to deliver from the stain of sins all them that hymn thee.[^1899]

*Troparion 2:* In the manner of the psalm, O Pure One, as queen thou didst stand at the right hand of the King Who shone forth from thy womb; entreat Him, O All-Spotless One, that He may show me forth standing at His right hand in the day of creation.[^1900]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having given birth unto the Heavenly Rain, thou didst renew the nature of mankind utterly dried up by improper deeds. Therefore I pray thee: Show the parched furrow of my soul fruitful, O Bride of God.[^1901]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Slain by the plant of knowledge, by the Tree of Life, O Pure One, we were called unto eternal life through Christ God, Who blossomed forth beyond understanding from thee, O Theotokos. Entreat Him with boldness that our souls may be saved.[^1902]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* The desert hath blossomed as a lily, O Lord, the barren Church of the nations by Thy coming, wherein my heart is established.[^1903]

*Troparion 1:* Having come forth from thy womb, O Most Pure One, the Creator clothed Himself in me, a man, granting a garment of incorruption unto him that was stripped naked by many evil deeds.[^1904]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst bear the priceless God the Word, O Sovereign Lady! Entreat Him diligently to have compassion on my humble soul which mourns because of shameful pleasures.[^1905]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Heal the wounds of my soul, O Most Pure One, and with thine effective medicine cure my humble heart embittered by the venom of the serpent.[^1906]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* As the Mother having boldness toward thy Son, O Sovereign Lady, ask help for the afflicted people, and cast down the haughtiness of the lawless.[^1907]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou didst come from the Virgin, not an ambassador, nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and didst save me, the whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1908]

*Troparion 1:* Rain down upon me a drop of compunction, O Sovereign Lady, taking away all the burning heat of my heart, and my sorrow, and driving away harmful assaults.[^1909]

*Troparion 2:* Wounded by the weapon of pleasure and lying in sores, despise me not, O Most Pure One, but heal me...[^1910]

*On Thursday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 2 (continued):* ...by the spear and blood of Thy crucified Son and our God.[^1911]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Enriched with every creation of the Master, account me, who am grievously impoverished, worthy of divine grace, that I may magnify thee as my good protectress, O All-Spotless One.[^1912]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Christ, the Father's Radiance, shone forth from thy womb, O Unwedded Maiden, and being crucified enlightened the universe, and destroyed the demonic darkness.[^1913]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou wast a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Source of Light.[^1914]

*Troparion 1:* Having given birth unto the Way of life, O Most Pure One, direct me now into the right path, who have been foolishly cast down into pathlessness and onto the shore of grievous falls.[^1915]

*Troparion 2:* Having senselessly alienated myself from the knowledge of God, I lived prodigally, wandering in the far country of passions; but turn me back, O Pure Virgin, and save me by thy consolation.[^1916]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* With thy living waters give drink unto thy servant, who am inflamed with the fire of sin and scorched by demonic assaults, O Virgin Mother most pure.[^1917]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Behold, in thy womb, O Most Pure Theotokos, thou didst hold Christ God beyond word, even as Isaiah proclaimed, and beyond nature didst bear Him, O Parent of God.[^1918]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I invoke the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: from corruption, O God, bring me up.[^1919]

*Troparion 1:* Show me not forth as a joy unto demons at the future judgment seat, O Sovereign Lady! But looking favorably upon me, entreat the Judge and thy Son.[^1920]

*Troparion 2:* Having provoked Thee to anger by thoughts, O Lord, and by my deceitful and unclean deeds, I bring Thy Mother in prayer unto Thee: show compassion and save me.[^1921]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Deliver me from condemnation, O Sovereign Lady, who am self-condemned by transgressions, since thou didst give birth unto the Judge and God of all, O All-Hymned One.[^1922]

*On Thursday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Entreat Jesus the Savior, Whom thou didst supernaturally bear in the flesh, O Virgin Mother most pure, that thy servants may be delivered from tribulations.[^1923]

*Then: Lord, have mercy (Thrice). Glory... both now...*[^1924]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Милосе́рдїа сꙋ́щи):*
The Virgin and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Tree, weeping bitterly, said: O my Son, what is this strange mystery? Thou Who bestowest eternal life upon all, how of Thine own will dost Thou die upon the Cross an ignominious death?[^1925]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The godless decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew upon the pious children, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.[^1926]

*Troparion 1:* Being my strength, and song, and salvation, and firm protection, and an unconquerable wall, O Sovereign Lady: conquer the demons that war against me, who ever seek to put me to death.[^1927]

*Troparion 2:* Having incarnated God by thy virginal blood, O Virgin, thou didst deify humanity. Therefore I pray thee: by thine intercessions deliver me who am defiled by passions and corrupted by the wiles of the enemy.[^1928]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* The furnace prefigured thy childbearing, O All-Spotless One! For it burned not the children, even as the unapproachable fire burned not thy womb. Therefore we pray thee: Deliver thy servants from eternal fire.[^1929]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* A most pure conception and incorrupt childbearing thou alone didst manifest, remaining a Virgin; for thou didst conceive Christ, O Pure One, God over all, become man for the salvation and deliverance of the faithful.[^1930]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its operation at the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful as they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^1931]

*Troparion 1:* Emulate the good, having departed from evil through care for divine deeds, O my soul, having the Parent of God praying for thee, and possessing her who is an unashamed protectress of all, as merciful and loving of goodness.[^1932]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst loose the human bond of the ancient condemnation. Therefore I pray thee, O Parent of God: loose every evil bond of my heart...[^1933]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 2 (continued):* ...binding me, O Most Pure One, with the divine love of the Creator.[^1934]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having given birth unto the Radiance of the Father's glory, O Theotokos, illuminate my heart mourning in the dishonor of transgressions, and show me forth a partaker of everlasting glory, that with faith I may glorify thee.[^1935]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* From thee, O Parent of God, the true Sun of Righteousness hath appeared unto us, enlightening all things with the beams of the Godhead: the Most High incarnate, Whom we hymn in song.[^1936]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* The Son of the Unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin hath appeared unto us, to enlighten them that are in darkness, and to gather the scattered; therefore do we magnify the all-hymned Theotokos.[^1937]

*Troparion 1:* Having tasted from the tree the food mingled with death, Adam found bitterness; but Thy Son, O Most Pure One, nailed upon the Tree, poured forth the sweetness of immortality: therefore do we praise thee.[^1938]

*Troparion 2:* Queen art thou, who beyond word didst bear Christ the King and Lord, Who demolished the kingdoms of hades. Diligently entreat Him, O Maiden, to count all them that honor thee worthy of the heavenly kingdom.[^1939]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Comfort my humble heart, O Sovereign Lady, afflicted by the enclosures of pleasure, as the Parent of the Good One, and being wholly good; and lead me into the good doors of repentance.[^1940]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Thou wast dead, lifted up upon the Cross; thereby Thou didst slay the serpent. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Have mercy on my soul slain by deceitful deeds, O Word, and quicken it through the prayers of her that bare Thee.[^1941]

*It is truly meet; Trisagion Prayers, and the rest.*[^1942]

*On Friday at Matins.*[^1943]
*After the First Stichology, Sessional Hymns of the Cross, Tone 2.*

*Sessional Hymn 1:* Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O Christ God, stretching out Thy most pure hands upon the Cross, gathering all the nations who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee![^1944]

*Verse: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and worship the footstool of His feet, for He is holy.*[^1945]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* In the very manner whereby the enemy made captive Adam through the tree of eating, in like manner, O Lord, hast Thou Thyself taken captive the enemy through the Tree of the Cross and through Thy Passion...[^1946]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Sessional Hymn 2 (continued):* ...for unto this art Thou come, the Second Adam, to seek him that was lost and to quicken him that was dead: O God, glory to Thee![^1947]

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion:* The Virgin and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Tree, weeping bitterly, said: O my Son, what is this strange mystery? Thou Who bestowest eternal life upon all, how of Thine own will dost Thou die upon the Cross an ignominious death?[^1948]

*After the Second Stichology, Sessional Hymns, Tone 2.*
*Sessional Hymn 1:* The Life-Giving Cross of Thy goodness, which Thou hast bestowed upon us the unworthy, O Lord, do we bring in supplication unto Thee: save the Emperor *(Name)*, and Thy city, granting peace, for the sake of the Theotokos, O sole Lover of mankind.[^1949]

*Verse: But God is our King of old: He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth.*[^1950]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* We worship Thine immaculate Icon, O Good One, asking forgiveness of our transgressions, O Christ God; for of Thine own will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver them whom Thou hadst created from the bondage of the enemy. Therefore thankfully do we cry unto Thee: Thou hast filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Who art come to save the world.[^1951]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^1952]

*For the Martyrs:* Having Thee, Who clothest the heaven with clouds, as their raiment in the world, the Saints endured torments from the lawless, and abolished the delusion of idols. By their prayers set us also free from the invisible enemy, O Savior, and save us.[^1953]

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion:* Standing by Thy Cross, she that bare Thee without seed, O Christ, unable to endure seeing Thee suffering unjustly, sobbed with weeping and cried out unto Thee: How dost Thou suffer, Who art impassible by nature, O sweetest Son? I hymn Thine extreme goodness.[^1954]

*After the Third Stichology, Sessional Hymns, Tone 2.*
*Sessional Hymn 1:* Like the thief do I make confession, and cry aloud unto Thee, O Good One: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom, and number me with him, Thou Who of Thine own will didst endure the passions for our sake.[^1955]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* Having enlightened earthly things by Thy Cross, and called sinners unto repentance: separate me not from Thy flock, O Good Shepherd! But seek me out who have gone astray, O Master, and number me with Thy hundredfold flock, for Thou art the sole Good One and Lover of mankind.[^1956]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Glory... both now... Stavrotheotokion:* Preserved by the Precious Cross of thy Son, O Sovereign Lady, pure Theotokos, we all easily conquer every assault of the adversary; therefore as is meet do we call thee blessed, as the Mother of God, and the sole hope of our souls.[^1957]

*Canon to the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.*[^1958]
*(Whose acrostic is: When the Cross was planted, demonic delusion fell.)*
*A composition of Joseph.*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having traversed an untrodden, unaccustomed, unwetted sea path, the chosen Israel cried aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified![^1959]

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst accept crucifixion, and wast ignominiously nailed with nails, O Word, desiring to bestow honor upon all men who glorify Thy voluntary sufferings.[^1960]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst stretch out Thy palms upon the Cross, O Savior, Who didst stretch out heaven like a curtain, wherewith Thou didst embrace the nations and peoples who glorify Thy voluntary passions.[^1961]

*For the Martyrs:* Taking the cross upon their shoulders, the passion-bearers fervently followed Christ Who was crucified, conforming themselves unto His divine passions.[^1962]

*For the Martyrs:* The angelic hosts sang praises, beholding your sufferings; but the multitude of demons wailed, O victorious martyrs most contemplative of God.[^1963]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* The word of the honorable Prophet is fulfilled: for a sword pierced thy heart, O Sovereign Lady, when thou didst behold thy Son nailed upon the Cross.[^1964]

*Another Canon, to the Most Holy Theotokos.*[^1965]
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt: for He is gloriously glorified.[^1966]

*Troparion 1:* Having given birth unto the Fountain of dispassion, O Maiden, heal me who am wounded by passions, and snatch me from eternal fire, O sole Rejoiced of God.[^1967]

*Troparion 2:* Deliver me from bodily sicknesses, and heal the improper passions of my soul, and snatch me from eternal fire, O sole Divine-Grace-bestowed One.[^1968]

*Troparion 3:* Under thy goodness do I now flee, O Virgin Mother most pure: deliver thy servant from sicknesses of soul, and passions...[^1969]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 3 (continued):* ...corruptive of the soul, and from eternal fire.[^1970]

*Troparion 4:* Thou art my fervent mercy-seat, O Sovereign Lady; unto thee do I run and am saved, and I obtain spiritual salvation: for thou canst save all, as being the Mother of God.[^1971]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit rejoiced to sing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous save Thee, O Lord![^1972]

*Troparion 1:* The incorrupt Cluster hanging upon the Tree dripped divine sweetness gladdening the hearts of men, destroying the drunkenness of malice: Jesus, the Redeemer of our souls.[^1973]

*Troparion 2:* Of Thine own will Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Jesus, and didst cast down all the evildoing of the devil; and men who had fallen into perdition through a corrupted mind hast Thou lifted up, O Many-Merciful One.[^1974]

*For the Martyrs:* Inflamed with the fire of divine love, the noble ones feared not the fire, neither were they terrified of death, hoping to receive the immortal gifts: unending joy and unwaning light.[^1975]

*For the Martyrs:* The passion-bearers dyed a most radiant purple robe in their own blood, and with it were clothed; and bearing in their right hand the divine Cross as a scepter, they reign ever with the Lord.[^1976]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* The ranks of the bodiless honor thee; for thou didst bear the Master of all in the flesh, Who through the Tree loosed all them that were bound, O Maiden, Bride of God, and bound the faithful unto His love.[^1977]

*Another [Canon], the Heirmos the same.*
*Troparion 1:* Thou didst bear the Unoriginate King, Who received flesh from thee, O Virgin Mother: Him therefore entreat as the Lover of mankind, to save thy servant from every affliction and from future condemnation.[^1978]

*Troparion 2:* Dispel the perplexity of my heart, heal my wounds, and drive away festering sores by thy divine power; and grant unto me a stream of compunction, O thou that didst bear the Ever-Living Fountain.[^1979]

*Troparion 3:* Heal my soul that is sick with demonic assaults and despondency, O Parent of God; and tears...[^1980]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 3 (continued):* ...of repentance grant unto my heart, and plant within it the fear of my Master, O Most Pure Lady.[^1981]

*Troparion 4:* Having spent my life in slothfulness, and having defiled my heart with passions, I come unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, with compunction of soul, and I pray: Have compassion and save me, establishing me in the ways of repentance.[^1982]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou hast come from the Virgin, not an intercessor, nor an angel, but Thou Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and hast saved me, the whole man. Therefore do I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1983]

*Troparion 1:* Thou wast suspended upon the Tree, O Almighty One, Who didst suspend the earth upon the waters; and pierced in the side with a spear, Thou didst pour forth blood with water unto the redemption of all.[^1984]

*Troparion 2:* When Thy side was pierced, my sickness was healed; when Thou wast struck on the cheek with a hand, I obtained freedom; and by the tasting of gall, O Christ, we were delivered from pleasurable food.[^1985]

*For the Martyrs:* Having wounded the deceitful serpent with your wounds, ye heal the wounds of our hearts at all times, pouring forth grace from the springs of the Savior, O divine martyrs.[^1986]

*For the Martyrs:* Ye inflicted a wound unto the whole body of the enemy, O all-honored ones, being bloodied with wounds, stretched out upon the cross, and flayed, O passion-bearers of divine countenance.[^1987]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* The Most High took flesh from thy most pure blood; and beholding Him unjustly lifted up upon the Tree, O Most Pure Lady, thou didst groan in tears and magnify His loving-kindness.[^1988]

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:* I sing to Thee; for by hearing, O Lord, have I heard and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who am lost. Therefore do I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Many-Merciful One.[^1989]

*Troparion 1:* Truly do I praise thee, O All-Lauded Lady, who beyond nature didst give birth unto the All-Praised God the Word; and I pray: Heal the sicknesses of my humble soul, and deliver me from grievous condemnation.[^1990]

*Troparion 2:* Habitually dost thou bedew us with thy rich mercies, O Virgin, soothing infirmities and breaking the diverse passions of the soul. Therefore loose the sinful bonds and many afflictions of my heart.[^1991]

*Troparion 3:* I have defiled my soul with passions; do thou, who wast...[^1992]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 3 (continued):* ...a most pure habitation unto the Most Pure One, cleanse me, O Parent of God, guiding me unto the light of repentance and snatching me from the fire to come.[^1993]

*Troparion 4:* Enlighten my mind, O most pure Sovereign Lady, I pray thee; calm the waves of my passion-tossed heart, destroying carnal desires, and bringing me unto the divine haven.[^1994]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* The Coal revealed unto Isaiah, the Sun, shone forth from the virginal womb, granting the enlightenment of the knowledge of God unto them that were lost in darkness.[^1995]

*Troparion 1:* For the sake of mercy Thou didst partake of the Cross, O Master, and from the depth of evils Thou didst pluck me, and didst honor me by seating me together with the Father, having willingly undergone dishonor.[^1996]

*Troparion 2:* Crowned with thorns, O Thou Who crownest all the earth with flowers, Thou cuttest down the thorns of my passions by the root, O Word, and plantest within me Thy understanding.[^1997]

*For the Martyrs:* Willingly clothing yourselves in strength through your infirmity, O holy martyrs, and being fortified, ye utterly destroyed the power of the demons.[^1998]

*For the Martyrs:* Having contended greatly upon earth, O holy ones, ye found great glory in the heavens, and ye deliver us from great afflictions who honor you.[^1999]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* He Who in a manner befitting God is borne upon the shoulders of the cherubim in the heavens, truly seated upon thy hands, O Most Pure Lady, was crucified and delivered all from corruption.[^2000]

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:* O Enlightenment of them that lie in darkness, Salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior, unto Thee I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy brightness, for I know no other God save Thee.[^2001]

*Troparion 1:* Quicken me who have been slain by the snares and traps of the deceitful one, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady Theotokos, who didst bear the hypostatic Life of all, that in godliness I may sing to thee, the all-lauded one.[^2002]

*Troparion 2:* O Virgin Mother, who didst show forth the Lamb and Shepherd, shepherd me who wander in my ways, and vouchsafe me on the Day of Judgment to be numbered among the sheep on the right hand, that I may sing of thy saving grace.[^2003]

*Troparion 3:* From the darkening of passions, from stumbling-blocks abounding through the assaults of the stranger, and from the future eternal torments of them that sin, deliver me, O Maiden, by thy supplications, I pray.[^2004]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 4:* O Bride of God, into whom the one Divine Word entered, enlightening all the inhabited earth, make the dawn of true repentance to shine upon me; illuminate me with saving rays, dispersing the darkness of my passions by thy supplications, I pray.[^2005]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Having heard the voice of suppliant words from an afflicted soul, O Master, deliver me from grievous things; for Thou alone art the Cause of our salvation.[^2006]

*Troparion 1:* Having given Thy back to stripes, and Thy cheeks to blows, and Thy face, O Savior, to spitting, Thou didst save me who have sinned greatly against Thee, in knowledge and in ignorance.[^2007]

*Troparion 2:* Even as a Lamb wast Thou led to slaughter, O Christ my God, bringing back again unto life them that were slain by the venomous biting of the noetic wolf: Glory to Thy crucifixion![^2008]

*For the Martyrs:* Keeping the laws of the Master, O martyrs, ye completely turned away from the lawless counsel of the transgressors, and having died, ye received the life to come.[^2009]

*For the Martyrs:* Joyfully arraying yourselves against the adversary powers, O holy ones, ye conquered them with divine weapons, and received crowns of victory from God.[^2010]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* That He might deify man, God is born of thee, O Virgin, and is crucified, and tastes death, slaying by the Cross him that of old had slain me.[^2011]

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:* Rolling in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy mercy: From corruption, O God, bring me up.[^2012]

*Troparion 1:* Now do I flee unto thee, O All-Lauded Lady, save me by thy prayers and preserve me; for whatsoever thou desirest, thou canst do, as the Mother of Him Who strengthens all things.[^2013]

*Troparion 2:* Tossed by the tempest of sorrows, and overwhelmed by the onsets of churning waves, save me, thy servant, O Virgin Theotokos.[^2014]

*Troparion 3:* Vouchsafe me thy mercy, who am held fast by mercilessness and malice; and from the torments lying before me, and from the eternal fire, snatch me away.[^2015]

*Troparion 4:* Having conceived the most pure Lamb Who takes away the sins of the world, thou didst bear Him, O All-Immaculate Lady; cease not to pray unto Him to grant me the forgiveness of sins.[^2016]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The God-opposing command of the lawless tyrant lifted up a high flame, but Christ spread spiritual dew over the godly youths, He Who is blessed and supremely praised.[^2017]

*Troparion 1:* Having been lifted up upon the Cross, O Word, Who art the Resurrection and Co-elevation of all, Thou didst raise me up who was fallen through transgression, and didst cast down the adversary who cast us down, showing him to be utterly impotent and dead: Glory to Thy dominion![^2018]

*Troparion 2:* With nails Thou didst nail the sins of the forefather, O Christ, and struck with a reed, Thou didst write freedom for all men: Glory to Thy suffering, whereby we were delivered from the darkness of passions![^2019]

*For the Martyrs:* Cleaved upon your bodies by abominable murderous hands, O fair-victorious great martyrs of Christ, ye remained in spirit inseparable from God, cutting down with the sword of courage and slaying the deceitful enemy.[^2020]

*For the Martyrs:* Having the invincible strength of Christ Who was crucified, the undefeated army utterly destroyed the destructive host; and having suffered, it received as a crown of victory the blessed and imperishable life.[^2021]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* Thou hast shown thyself to be the animate palace of the King, O Virgin, and His fiery-formed throne, sitting upon which He raised up all men from their first fall, and honored them by seating them together with the Father.[^2022]

*Another [Canon], the Heirmos the same.*
*Troparion 1:* O thou that didst give birth unto the hypostatic Life for us, Who by death visibly abolished death, put to death the passions of my heart, and grant me a fountain of tears, O Pure Lady, that I may ever glorify thee.[^2023]

*Troparion 2:* O hope that puts not to shame, sure expectation and unassailable rampart, be thou a protection and helper unto me, O All-Immaculate One, who hope in thee; and guide me, O Pure Lady, unto the light of repentance and compunction.[^2024]

*Troparion 3:* Entreat Thy Son that Thy servant may be delivered from every demonic malice, and sorrow, and condemnation, and eternal fire, that in faith I may ever glorify thee.[^2025]

*Troparion 4:* A most pure conception and an incorrupt birthgiving hast thou alone shown forth, having remained a Virgin; for thou didst conceive Christ, O Pure One, God over all, Who became man...[^2026]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 4 (continued):* ...unto the salvation and redemption of the faithful.[^2027]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Disregarding the golden image, the thrice-blessed youths, beholding the immutable and living image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all creation that has been brought into being praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2028]

*Troparion 1:* A disobedient people, performing all lawless deeds without fear, lifted Thee up and crucified Thee upon the Tree between two lawless ones, O Compassionate One, Who justifiest the lawless; yet all creation glorifies Thee as Lord and Master, singing of Thy longsuffering.[^2029]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst bloody Thy fingers, O Christ, when nailed unto the Tree, and didst cause to cease the blood offered of old unto demons unto the destruction of them that offered it; therefore all creation glorifies Thee, singing of Thy love for mankind, O God of all.[^2030]

*For the Martyrs:* Being filled with living waters, the martyrs dried up the deceitful torrents by the divine streams of their blood, crying with unwavering faith: Let all creation that has been brought into being praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2031]

*For the Martyrs:* The countless multitude of your blood, O holy ones, extinguished the fire of impiety and destroyed the polytheistic delusion of the Hellenes; and it enlightened all the faithful who sing: Let all creation praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages![^2032]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* The Spotless Ewe-Lamb, the adornment of the prophets and martyrs, beholding Thee, the Lamb, lifted up upon the Tree, O Unoriginate Word, wept bitterly and said: Let all creation that has been brought into being praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages![^2033]

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:* Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2034]

*Troparion 1:* O refuge and helper of Christians, despise me not who am held in grievous things, O most pure Virgin, compassed about ever by tribulations, and by many assaults of the wicked demons.[^2035]

*Troparion 2:* Forget not the voice of thy suppliants, O awesome Protectress, but from every affliction and from every threatening snatch us away by thy supplications; for...[^2036]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 2 (continued):* ...thy maternal prayer inclines God to mercy.[^2037]

*Troparion 3:* Quench now the fierce storm of my passions, O pure and blessed Lady, and conquer, O Maiden, all the bodiless foes who mercilessly assault my poverty, that in faith I may sing to thee.[^2038]

*Troparion 4:* From the furnace of temptations, from the flame of sins, and from the fire of passions and Gehenna, and from demonic assault at the hour of my end, snatch me away, O sole Parent of God, Protectress of the faithful.[^2039]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee as is meet, and an intellect even of the transcendent world is dazed to sing to thee, O Theotokos; yet since thou art good, accept our faith, for thou knowest our divine love; for thou art the Protectress of Christians: thee do we magnify.[^2040]

*Troparion 1:* That Isaac might prefigure of old Thy Passion, he was bound, O Word! But the bound ram in the same figure in the bush of Sabek was untied for remission, and an involuntary sacrifice was truly dismissed; for when Thou wast willingly sacrificed, we were loosed from evils.[^2041]

*Troparion 2:* Being comely in beauty beyond the sons of men, O Christ, yet having neither beauty nor form in Thy Passion, suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, Thou didst adorn the ugliness of all the human race: Glory to Thy loving-kindness, O only merciful Lord![^2042]

*For the Martyrs:* Ye were revealed as divine citizens of Zion on high, and martyrs equal in honor with the angels as fellow-citizens; and ye radiantly enlighten the Church of the firstborn, O holy ones, shining with divine light and adorned with crowns of martyrdom.[^2043]

*For the Martyrs:* O beloved friends of Him Who gloriously loved you, deliver me from deceitful friendship with the flesh, O holy martyrs of the Lord! And ask sanctification, enlightenment, and remission of grievous transgressions for all them that celebrate your memory.[^2044]

*Theotokion [Stavrotheotokion]:* The beauty of the luminaries gave way from its customary course when they beheld Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, willingly lifted up upon the Cross; and the Virgin with the Virgin Disciple cried out with weeping: Woe is me! exclaiming: What is this strange sight?[^2045]

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:* The Son of the Unoriginate Parent, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us...[^2046]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Heirmos (continued):* ...to enlighten them that were darkened, and to gather together them that were scattered: Therefore the all-lauded Theotokos do we magnify.[^2047]

*Troparion 1:* Beyond mind and word didst thou give birth unto the Lover of mankind, the only many-merciful Lord; Him entreat, O Virgin, at the dread hour of judgment, to deliver thy servant from the eternal fire.[^2048]
