*Troparion 2:* Unto us who sing to thee, and glorify thee in faith, and ever flee unto thy divine protection, grant from heaven deliverance from grievous and tormenting passions, O Bride of God, and deliver us from torment and judgment.[^2049]

*Troparion 3:* Fair to behold, but in tasting most bitter, was the fruit of sin that slew me, wherewith I was filled unto satiety, and I anticipate the dreadful judgment: snatch me away from it, O most holy Virgin Mother.[^2050]

*Troparion 4:* Sweeten my humble heart, O Most Pure Lady, embittered by pleasurable bondages, as the Parent of the Good One, being wholly good; and lead me unto the good gates of repentance.[^2051]

*Then, "It is truly meet:" Litany, Exapostilarion, and the customary Psalms.*[^2052]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 2:*[^2053]

*Sticheron 1:* The Tree of Thy Cross, O Christ God, hast Thou shown forth as a tree of life unto us who believe in Thee, and thereby having brought to nought the power of him that had the dominion of death, Thou didst quicken us who were slain by sin. Therefore do we cry unto Thee: O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee![^2054]

*Verse:* We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord: [and we rejoiced and were glad. And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works,] and guide their sons.[^2055]

*Sticheron 2:* Willingly didst Thou become poor, on account of Adam's poverty, O Christ God; Thou camest upon the earth, incarnate of the Virgin, and didst receive crucifixion, that Thou mightest free us from the slavery of the enemy: Lord, glory to Thee![^2056]

*Verse:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us; and direct Thou the works of our hands over us; yea, the work of our hands direct Thou.[^2057]

*For the Martyrs:* Having suffered for Christ even unto death, O passion-bearing martyrs, ye have your souls in the heavens in the hand of God, and throughout the whole world your relics are venerated; priests and kings bow down, and all people rejoicing cry out in harmony: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones![^2058]

*On Friday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда̀ отъ дре́ва Тѧ̀):*[^2059]
O Thy goodness! O loving-kindness surpassing intellect! O Thy abundant bounty and longsuffering, O pre-unoriginate Word! the Virgin cried out, weeping: How hast Thou, Who art immortal, loved to die? A great mystery do I behold; yet I worship Thy Passion, which Thou didst willingly endure.[^2060]

*It is good to give thanks unto the Lord: Trisagion. After "Our Father:" troparion and litanies. Then the First Hour.*[^2061]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes, Tone 2:*[^2062]
The voice of the thief do we bring unto Thee, and we pray: Remember us, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom.[^2063]

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst uproot the wicked thorn, O Master, having willingly worn a crown of thorns, O longsuffering One.[^2064]

*Troparion 2:* Being crucified upon the Place of the Skull, O Sinless One, Thou didst crush the head of the evil one, and didst save all men.[^2065]

*For the Martyrs:* Crushed, O martyrs, ye crushed all the power of the enemy, and received crowns of victory.[^2066]

*Glory...*
*Triadikon:* Enlightened by the sprinkling of the divine blood, we the faithful honor one Godhead in three Persons.[^2067]

*Both now...*
*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Christ as a Lamb suspended upon the Tree, the All-Immaculate Lady, sobbing with tears, magnified Him.[^2068]

## Saturday: All Saints and the Departed[^2069]

*On Friday Evening.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerables:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда̀ отъ дре́ва Тѧ̀):*[^2070]

*Sticheron 1:* Delivering your flesh to wounds, and enduring most bitter torments and violent death, O all-lauded martyrs, ye put the tormentors to shame, and truly abolished idolatrous honors, proclaiming Christ as the one God and Master, before Whom, with the angelic choirs, ye glorious crown-bearers stand. *(Twice.)*[^2071]

*On Friday Evening, Tone 2.*

*Sticheron 2:* Ye were revealed as divine preachers of the Word Who appeared upon earth, and ye taught all godliness, setting forth the Orthodox exposition of divine doctrines, whereby ye drove heresy far away from the Church of Christ. Therefore, as sacred ministers of the Trinity, bring all into the ever-living mansions, O blessed ones.[^2072]

*Other Stichera, for the Martyrs, Same Tone:*[^2073]

*Sticheron 1:* Having not loved earthly delights, the passion-bearers were accounted worthy of heavenly good things, and became fellow-citizens of the angels: O Lord, by their prayers have mercy on us and save us.[^2074]

*Sticheron 2:* While the holy martyrs pray for us and sing to Christ, all delusion has ceased, and the human race is saved by faith.[^2075]

*Sticheron 3:* The choirs of the martyrs resisted the tormentors, saying: We are soldiers of the King of hosts; though ye deliver us to fire and torments, we deny not the power of the Trinity.[^2076]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*
The shadow of the law passed away when grace arrived; for even as the bush burned not being consumed, so as a Virgin didst thou give birth, and a Virgin didst thou remain; instead of a pillar of fire, the Sun of Righteousness shone forth; instead of Moses, Christ, the Salvation of our souls.[^2077]

*Then, "O Joyful Light:" Prokeimenon of the day. "Vouchsafe, O Lord:"*[^2078]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*[^2079]

*For the Martyrs:* Great is the glory which ye acquired by faith, O holy ones; for not only in sufferings did ye conquer the enemy, but even after death ye drive away spirits and heal the sick, physicians of souls and bodies: Pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved.[^2080]

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.[^2081]

*For the Departed:* Like a flower man withers, and like a shadow he passes away; each man is dissolved. But again, when the trumpet sounds, the dead, as in an earthquake, shall all arise to meet Thee, O Christ God! Then, O Master, settle the spirits of Thy servants whom Thou hast translated from us in the tabernacles of Thy saints.[^2082]

*Verse:* Their souls shall dwell amid good things.[^2083]

*For the Departed:* Woe is me! what an agony has the soul when being separated from the body; woe is me! how greatly it weeps then, and there is none...[^2084]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*For the Departed (continued):* ...who shows mercy unto it; lifting up its eyes unto the angels, it prays in vain; stretching out its hands unto men, it has none to help. Therefore, my beloved brethren, having understood our brief life, let us ask rest from Christ for the departed, and for our souls great mercy.[^2085]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Save Thy servants from perils, O Virgin Theotokos; for next to God we all flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable rampart and protection.[^2086]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion; after "Our Father:" Troparion and dismissal.*[^2087]

*On Friday at Compline.*
*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*[^2088]

*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea, and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt; for He is glorified.[^2089]

*Refrain:* Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.[^2090]

*Troparion 1:* Thou art the fountain of life, O pure Virgin Mother, having given birth unto the Prince and Lord, the Life of all, bedewing them that in faith glorify thee.[^2091]

*Troparion 2:* Being enriched, we possess thee as a foundation and protectress, who confess thee to be the Theotokos, O Most Pure One, and from the surge of life's billows are we saved, O all-immaculate Virgin.[^2092]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* O thou that didst bear the Fountain of immortality, heal me who am wounded with passions, O Maiden, and snatch me from eternal fire, O sole Divine-Grace-bestowed One.[^2093]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* O refuge of the faithful, Ever-Virgin, and mighty help of them that flee unto thee, save us from every necessity and harm of the adversary.[^2094]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing to Thee.[^2095]

*Troparion 1:* Truly as the golden censer, and the jar of manna, and the divine mountain, and the exceedingly fair palace of God, do we name thee, O Virgin.[^2096]

*Troparion 2:* Being the temple and sacred dwelling of the Word, O Theotokos, be thou ever the cleansing of my transgressions, O most holy Virgin.[^2097]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Neither an earthly tongue nor an intellect of the bodiless powers is able to tell of thy childbearing; for beyond nature and understanding, O Theotokos, didst thou give birth unto the Creator.[^2098]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Be thou a confirmation, refuge, and protection, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unto them that in faith flee unto thee and confess thee to be the Mother of God.[^2099]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thy unsearchable power, O Lover of mankind.[^2100]

*Troparion 1:* We Christians have gained thee, O Theotokos, as a great help: snatch us away from grievous perils.[^2101]

*Troparion 2:* O unwedded Lady, who didst conceive God in Thy womb: deliver us all from tribulations and sorrows.[^2102]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* We the faithful have gained thee, O Pure One, as an invincible rampart and a mighty hope in tribulations, O Theotokos.[^2103]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Having gained thy prayer, O Lady, as a firm foundation, we are delivered from diverse afflictions.[^2104]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for besides Thee we know no other God.[^2105]

*Troparion 1:* Incarnate of thee, O Birthgiver of God, have we the faithful known the Son born without seed, true God and man by nature; therefore do we glorify thee.[^2106]

*Troparion 2:* Under thy shelter and protection, O Most Pure One, we the faithful, ever fleeing in faith, are delivered through thee, O Theotokos, from every grievous assault.[^2107]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Deliver us from perils and from the storm of thoughts, O Most Pure One, from all wrath, from every sin, from famine and pestilence, and from eternal torment, O Virgin.[^2108]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Being our Protectress, salvation, and hope, O Lady of Christians: save them that ever faithfully sing to thee with love, O all-hymned Virgin.[^2109]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy mercy: from corruption, O God, raise me up![^2110]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 1:* He Who by His will works all things, having by His will dwelt within the womb of the Unwedded One, hath enriched with incorruption them that were sick with corruption, as He is merciful.[^2111]

*Troparion 2:* Higher than the heavenly powers and more holy art thou, O All-Immaculate One, having contained in thy womb beyond nature the uncontainable Word.[^2112]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Lead me who have strayed upon the path of life, and who through lawlessness often fall into sins, O Lady, unto the paths of repentance.[^2113]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* Despise not, O Pure One, our entreaties, the servants who have placed our hope in thee; for thou art a refuge, O Lady, and the cleansing of souls.[^2114]

*Then: Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*
Thou didst conceive the Word without seed, and didst give birth unto the only Son, Christ; for thou didst bear as a new child Thine own Creator. Therefore do we magnify thee, O Theotokos.[^2115]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Unto the golden image worshipped in the plain of Dura, Thy three youths paid no heed, disregarding the godless command; but cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^2116]

*Troparion 1:* The noetic Son, consubstantial with the Father, hast thou incarnated by the power of the All-Holy Spirit, O Lady graced of God; Him, therefore, pray unceasingly to show compassion unto them that sing: Blessed art thou who didst give birth unto God in the flesh![^2117]

*Troparion 2:* O pure, unwedded, holy, and blessed Virgin, the restoration of the falling, the deliverance of them that sin! save me, a prodigal, save me, crying unto thy Son: Blessed art thou who didst give birth unto God in the flesh![^2118]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* A firm haven, an awesome protectress, and an impregnable rampart unto them that are in distress through necessities and tossed by the tempest in sorrow: by thy prayers unto thy Son, being the Theotokos, save thy servants from manifold temptations.[^2119]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* O thou sole hope and help of the faithful, Birthgiver of God, make haste to help thy servants who are submerged by sorrows, at a loss on every side, who are in afflictions, and who flee unto thee with love of soul.[^2120]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew: sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2121]

*Troparion 1:* Being a living fountain, having given birth unto the Water of life, bedew my soul that is melted by the flame of sin, O Virgin Theotokos, that I may glorify thee unto all ages.[^2122]

*Troparion 2:* Them that were put to death and brought down unto mortal and corruptible dust didst thou alone raise up, having given birth unto the Prince of life, Christ our God, O Lady, Virgin pure and blessed.[^2123]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Deliver me, O pure Lady, from eternal fire and condemnation, and snatch me away from evil-doing men and them that seek to entrap me, that I may ever call thee blessed, whom all creation divinely blesses.[^2124]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* In the likeness of flesh was the Pre-divine One seen from thee, O pure Virgin! Him pray unceasingly to show mercy unto us who live in malice and tremble at the eternal torment.[^2125]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* God the Word from God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, and Who was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin for our sake: with one mind in hymns do we the faithful magnify Him.[^2126]

*Troparion 1:* Vouchsafe me divine love for mankind, O Maiden, who alone didst ineffably give birth unto God, the Lover of mankind, Who borrowed flesh from thee; and deliver me from future flame and from every torment, who glorify thee in love.[^2127]

*Troparion 2:* Having gained thee as a mighty protectress, as hope and rampart, and foundation and firm protection, and invincible confirmation and calm haven, and sovereign refuge: we all are saved, O All-Hymned One.[^2128]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* O Virgin, Birthgiver of Light, drive away the clouds of my soul, and grant me purely to behold the saving beauty, O Lady, which shone forth ineffably from thy most holy womb as a light unto the nations, O All-Hymned One.[^2129]

*Both now...*
*Troparion 4:* O thou who didst bear the Divine Light, enlighten my heart that is darkened by the onslaught of many passions and alienating thoughts, O Maiden, giving me a drop...[^2130]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 2.*

*Troparion 4 (continued):* ...ever granting tears, O Virgin, which cleanse the defilement of sin.[^2131]

*Then: It is truly meet; Trisagion; and after "Our Father:" the remainder as usual, and dismissal.*[^2132]

*On Saturday at Matins.*
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymns of the Martyrs, Tone 2.*

Having Thee as a garment in the world, Who clothest heaven with clouds, the saints endured tortures from lawless ones, and abolished the deception of idols. By their prayers free us also, O Savior, from the invisible enemy, and save us.[^2133]

*Verse:* Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.[^2134]

Apostles, Martyrs, and Prophets, Hierarchs, Venerables, and Righteous ones, who have fought the good fight and kept the faith, having boldness before the Savior: pray Him as good, we beseech you, on our behalf, that our souls may be saved.[^2135]

*Verse:* Their souls shall dwell amid good things.[^2136]

*Tone 2, Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Милосе́рдїѧ сꙋ́щи):*
As God ruling over both the dead and the living, possessing authority over all, O Giver of life, receive the supplication of Thy servants; show Thy mercy, O Lover of mankind, and grant remission unto the souls which in hope in Thee Thou hast translated, for the sake of Thy tender-heartedness, as Thou art good.[^2137]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
All beyond understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Theotokos! Though sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast known to be an unfeigned Mother, having given birth unto the true God: Him pray, that our souls may be saved.[^2138]

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymns, Tone 2.*

O Thou Who hast enlightened Thy saints more than gold, and hast glorified Thy venerable ones as Thou art good: being entreated by them, O Christ God, bring peace unto our life as Lover of mankind, and direct our prayer as incense, O Thou Who alone restest in the saints.[^2139]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*

O passion-bearers of the Lord! Blessed is the earth that was drenched with your blood, and holy are the shrines that received your bodies; for at the tribunal ye convicted the enemy, and with boldness preached Christ: Pray Him as good, we beseech you, that our souls may be saved.[^2140]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.[^2141]

Remember Thy servants, O Lord, as Thou art good, and forgive whatever sins they have committed in this life; for no one is sinless, save Thee alone, Who art able to give rest also unto the departed.[^2142]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, Virgin Mary, who didst give birth unto Christ the Redeemer and Savior: pray His goodness together with the Apostles and Martyrs and Prophets, with the Venerables and Hieromartyrs, to grant us remission of sins and great mercy.[^2143]

*Then we chant the Canon in the Menaion unto the Saint. After this we chant the Canon unto the Saint whose temple it is. But if it be a temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, the temple canon takes precedence. This order is observed on all Saturdays of the Tones, outside of a feast.*[^2144]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and for the Departed.*
*(Whose acrostic is: Unto God's close servants do I bring praise. Joseph.)*[^2145]

*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having taken up the Mosaic song, cry out, O my soul: He was unto me a helper and a protector unto salvation; this is my God, and I will glorify Him![^2146]

*Refrain: Holy martyrs, pray unto God for us.*
*For the Martyrs:* Having patiently endured grievous banishments and sore wounds, O sufferers, by divine power ye drove away all deception from the ends of the earth.[^2147]

*Refrain: Hierarchs of Christ, pray unto God for us.*
*For the Hierarchs:* Ministers and Hierarchs of God, shining manifestly with noetic light, guided the fullness of all the pious unto the light of piety.[^2148]

*Refrain: All ye saints, pray unto God for us.*
*For the Venerables:* Having humbled your proud mind, O Venerable ones, ye came unto the good land; and being exalted by divine forms, ye ever help all the humble.[^2149]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*
*For the Departed:* As God, show forth as partakers of the most radiant light and eternal gladness Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast translated from temporal things, O our most good God.[^2150]

*Theotokion:* Having valiantly and patiently contested by fasting, the venerable women cast down the enemy, and with thee, O Birthgiver of God, they stand rejoicing.[^2151]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*Second Canon for the Departed, chanted in order where there is no Menaion.*
*(Whose acrostic is: For the dead a second hymn do I weave.) A work of Theophanes.*[^2152]

*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Unto Him Who by His divine command dried up the impassable billowing sea, and guided the Israelite people to go on foot through it, let us sing unto the Lord; for He is gloriously glorified.[^2153]

*Refrain: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*
*Troparion 1:* Trampling down death by Thy death, Thou didst pour forth the ever-existing flow of divine life; grant this, O Good One, unto the souls of the departed, bestowing remission of transgressions through the entreaties of Thy martyrs.[^2154]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*
*Troparion 2:* O Thou Who ever pourest forth rich mercy unto them that ever piously receive Thee in Thy house, O Christ, in Thy marvelous tabernacle: grant a place of refreshment, O Master, unto Thy servants, as Thou art tender-hearted.[^2155]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou wast mightier than death, O Christ; therefore, having bound it, Thou didst deliver us; and now deliver the departed from this corruption, as Thou art good, and grant them communion in Thy radiance.[^2156]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Having established my wavering mind, O Mother of God, strengthen me by the divine commandments of Him Who was born of thy consecrated womb, and Who abolished, O Lady, the gloomy kingdom of Hades.[^2157]

*Canon 1. Ode 3. Heirmos:* My barren mind show Thou fruitful, O God, Husbandman of noble things and Planter of good things, by Thy tender-heartedness.[^2158]

*Refrain: Holy martyrs, pray unto God for us.*
*For the Martyrs:* Having been ignited with the fire of Christ's love, O passion-bearers, ye quenched the burning of tortures by the dewing of the All-Working Spirit.[^2159]

*Refrain: Hierarchs of Christ, pray unto God for us.*
*For the Hierarchs:* O most holy Hierarchs of Christ, and venerable assemblies of the monastics: entreat God, the Lover of mankind, on behalf of us all.[^2160]

*Refrain: All ye saints, pray unto God for us.*
*For the Prophets and Holy Women:* The most sacred choir of the divine prophets was magnified, and the multitude of women who valiantly suffered attained glory.[^2161]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*
*For the Departed:* Being put to death upon the Cross, O Christ, Thou didst bestow im-...[^2162]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Departed (continued):* ...-mortality unto the dead; which grant them to obtain, who in faith have departed unto Thee.[^2163]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Together with all the Prophets and the consecrated women, now earnestly entreat God Who was born of thee, O Virgin, to show compassion unto us.[^2164]

*Canon 2. Ode 3. Heirmos:* Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth over mine enemies; for my spirit hath rejoiced when singing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^2165]

*Troparion 1:* Vouchsafe them that in faith have fallen asleep to be illuminated with the radiance of Thy beauty together with Thy martyrs, O Thou Who art rich in mercy; for Thou art our God, and none is righteous besides Thee, O Lord.[^2166]

*Troparion 2:* In the place of refreshment, in the bosom of Thine elect Abraham, grant Thy servants to dwell, as Thou art compassionate, who cry unto Thee: Thou art our God, and none is righteous besides Thee, O Lord.[^2167]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Receive Thy servants bearing lamps to dwell in the heavenly bridal chambers, having entered together with the wise virgins, O Master, whom by Thy will Thou hast translated from temporal things, O only Lover of mankind.[^2168]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Me who was deadened and returned unto the earth hast thou raised up, having given birth unto the Giver of life; and thou hast brought me up from the lowest Hades, who in faith glorify thee as Theotokos and venerate thee, O most pure and all-hymned Lady.[^2169]

*Canon 1. Ode 4. Heirmos:* Foreseeing Thy birth of the Virgin, the Prophet proclaimed, crying: I heard Thy report, and was afraid; for from the south, from the holy and shaded mountain, art Thou come, O Christ![^2170]

*Troparion 1:* Being well likened unto Christ's sufferings, the glorious passion-bearers, stretched out under many tortures, rejoiced looking unto the eternal rewards; which having received, they are ever called blessed.[^2171]

*Troparion 2:* Keeping the laws of the Spirit, O high priests, ye most wisely shepherded the people, as excellent pilots, in the divine harbor; and having transferred from worldly turmoils, ye passed over unto the quietude of life.[^2172]

*Troparion 3:* Strangers were ye revealed upon the earth, O fathers, having transferred your life unto the heavens, as it is written, devout-...[^2173]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Venerables (continued):* ...-ly wise, and having curbed the carnal passions through the pains of self-restraint by the power of Christ.[^2174]

*For the Departed:* Whomsoever Thou hast translated, O Master, from temporal life—a numerous multitude that served Thee in the Orthodox faith, O Word—uniting them with the multitude of all the saved, vouchsafe them eternal life.[^2175]

*Glory...*
*For the Holy Women:* Desiring divine mortification which truly mediates endless life, by reason of tortures and afflictions and acts of abstinence the honorable women attained unto it, praying Christ and God on our behalf.[^2176]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* The Prophet Habakkuk, O Theotokos, sees thee as a mountain overshadowed with virtues, from which God ineffably appeared, covering the heavens with His virtue and saving the human race from corruption.[^2177]

*Canon 2. Ode 4. Heirmos:* I sing to Thee, for I heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; for unto me dost Thou come, seeking me who had strayed. Therefore Thy great condescension toward me do I glorify, O Plenteous in mercy![^2178]

*Troparion 1:* Them that have lived in hope and love and with an Orthodox mind vouchsafe Thy glory which surpasses the mind, O Christ, through Thy manifold love for mankind and through the entreaties of the chosen martyrs.[^2179]

*Troparion 2:* As Thou possessest an ever-flowing torrent of sweetness, Thou ever givest drink unto the elect, O Lord! With them also now feed those translated unto Thee, O Christ, by Thine ineffable mercy beside the waters of forgiveness.[^2180]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou art Lord over the living, O Master, and rulest over the dead; Thou by Thy power shalt resurrect the dust that is in the earth. Therefore settle those who have come unto Thee in Thy courts, O Savior.[^2181]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Thou hast healed Eve's crushing and ancient misery; for thou didst give birth unto the Creator Who is able to restore us who were cast down by transgressions, O Birthgiver of God, sole Virgin Mother.[^2182]

*Canon 1. Ode 5. Heirmos:* Having dispersed the gloom of my soul, O my Savior, enlighten me with the light of Thy commandments, as the only King of peace.[^2183]

*Troparion 1:* Having loved Thee, O Compassionate One, the noble passion-bearers hated all worldly things, mingling with tortures through renunciation of the flesh.[^2184]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Hierarchs & Monastics:* Hierarchs, Prophets, venerable God-bearers, with spiritual rays ye enlighten the world, dispelling the darkness of passions.[^2185]

*For All Saints:* Venerable Fathers, Prophets, High Priests, and ever-memorable women, earnestly entreat Thee, the Master of all, on our behalf.[^2186]

*Glory...*
*For the Departed:* We pray Thee, O Word: having numbered with the choir of Thine elect those whom Thou hast taken from us, show them forth as partakers of the better life.[^2187]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* O most pure Virgin Mother, the praise of Martyrs, Venerables, and Righteous ones: snatch us away from every torment of the evil one.[^2188]

*Canon 2. Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for besides Thee we know no other God.[^2189]

*Troparion 1:* Us who were deadened and laid in corruption hast Thou brought up from the dark treasuries of Hades, and hast snatched away, O Good One, uniting us unto the armies of the holy angels.[^2190]

*Troparion 2:* Since Thou camest, O Christ, that Thou mightest save us: receive now those who have piously been translated unto Thee, as Thou art alone good, and settle them in the bosom of Abraham with Lazarus.[^2191]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou hast dissolved, O Master, my ancient and long-standing warfare, when Thou previously becamest a Mediator and Intercessor of reconciliation; and now having shown compassion unto Thy servants, give them rest.[^2192]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Under thy shelter, O Mother of God, they that hope in thee are saved; for thou didst give birth for us unto the Giver of life, Who quickens all things by His will.[^2193]

*Canon 1. Ode 6. Heirmos:* In the abyss of sin am I held, O my Savior, and in the deep of life am I tossed by the tempest. But as Jonah from the monster, lead me up also from the passions, and save me![^2194]

*Troparion 1:* By strength of heart the sufferers, having set themselves in array against the enemy, cast him down, and received from God crowns of victory; and now they earnestly pray for all earthborn mortals.[^2195]

*Troparion 2:* Let us faithfully honor the hierarchs of God, and call His venerable ones bles-...[^2196]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Hierarchs & Monastics (continued):* ...-sed, being saved by their entreaties from all wrath and affliction and assault of the enemy.[^2197]

*For the Holy Women:* The divine choir of women suffered, and in fasting was well-pleasing unto God, and obtained the Kingdom of heaven; by their prayers show compassion, O God, unto Thy world.[^2198]

*Glory... For the Departed:*
O Christ, Giver of life, Who didst fashion man from the earth: give rest unto those whom Thou hast translated from us, granting them remission of evil deeds, as Thou art merciful and Lover of mankind.[^2199]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Holy Theotokos, sanctify our thoughts and strengthen our mind, and save us unwounded from the darts of the evil one, who glorify thy majesties, O All-Hymned One.[^2200]

*Canon 2. Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy mercy: from corruption, O God, raise me up![^2201]

*Troparion 1 (Twice):* Vouchsafe those whom Thou hast translated from the earth by Thy all-working will, O Lover of mankind, unto the ineffable and divine brightness where the ranks of martyrs abide.[^2202]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 2:* Vouchsafe those who have left this life to be illuminated by the beauty of Thy glory, and give rest unto them that have passed over unto Thine ineffable light, O Master.[^2203]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Earnestly appear delivering them that call upon thee, O most pure Lady, who didst give birth unto God Who hath dominion over life and death.[^2204]

*Canon 1. Ode 7. Heirmos:* Imitating the Cherubim, the youths in the furnace danced, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God, for in truth and judgment hast Thou brought all these things upon us for our sins, O supremely praised and supremely glorified unto all ages![^2205]

*Troparion 1:* By patient endurance the saints cast down Beliar, enduring every temptation of bitter torments, for truly they loved God Who suffered for our sins. By their prayers save us all, O Word, from perils and afflictions, who glorify Thee.[^2206]

*Troparion 2:* Radiant Hierarchs, and Venerables, and Prophets, and the glorious multitude of Hieromartyrs, and the consecrated assembly of holy women who shone forth valiantly in suffering and in fasting: ever make supplication unto God on our behalf, that He may have mercy upon us.[^2207]

*Troparion 3:* The multitude of martyrs entreats Thee, O Christ Benefactor: from all...[^2208]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Martyrs (continued):* ...of sorrows, and afflictions, and cruel temptations, and all transgressions, and from harm save me, O Word, who am perishing.[^2209]

*For the Departed:* Where pain and sighing and sorrow have fled away, where the light of Thy countenance shines, O Christ, and where the choirs of the Saints now dance: establish the souls of all who have passed away in faith, having overlooked all their transgressions, as Thou alone art merciful.[^2210]

*Theotokion:* With the Martyrs, O Most Pure One, and with the venerable Fathers, and all the Prophets, with the holy women, implore Him Who alone rests among the Saints, to sanctify us all who praise thee with holy voices, O Pure Lady, unto all ages.[^2211]

*Canon 2. Ode 7. Heirmos:* The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew upon the pious youths, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.[^2212]

*Troparion 1:* Thou Who art everywhere present didst descend, O Lord, to save the human race that was deceived of old. Therefore the martyrs pray Thee: give rest, O Savior, to those whom Thou hast translated from the earth into the land of the meek.[^2213]

*Troparion 2:* Thou hast shaken off the deadness of mortality, being free among the dead, Thou alone, O Christ! And now, O Master, deliver Thy servants from the deadness of sin, showing them forth as heirs of Thy Kingdom.[^2214]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* By Thy manifold and ineffable tender mercy, and by the boundless abyss of Thy love for mankind, O Christ, grant remission of sins unto the departed, and show them cleansed by Thy grace.[^2215]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* A lamp-stand wast thou, bearing the brightness of divine glory, O Virgin: Him Who appeared unto us in the flesh and destroyed the gloom of Hades by the splendor of His Divinity, O thou full of divine joy![^2216]

*Canon 1. Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him Who of old prefigured the miracle of the Virgin unto Moses in the bush on Mount Sinai: praise, bless, and supremely exalt unto all ages![^2217]

*For the Martyrs:* The stream of your blood, O passion-bearers of the Lord, hath sanctified all creation, and visibly dried up the outpouring of deception, and richly watered the souls of the faithful.[^2218]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Saints:* The fasting choir, the assembly of hierarchs, of holy women, and of glorious prophets was shown equal in honor unto the angels, having lived angelically on earth in the spirit.[^2219]

*For All Saints:* Deliver us all, O passion-bearers of the Lord, high priests, and prophets, and multitude of venerable ones, and holy women, from the darts of the enemy, who praise you.[^2220]

*Glory... For the Departed:*
Those whom Thou hast received from us, O Savior, establish in the bosom of Abraham, and give them rest with all the elect, granting remission of all their sins, as Thou art all-bountiful.[^2221]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure Mother of God, with all the holy prophets, and martyrs, and venerable ones, and holy women, and hieromartyrs: pray the Savior to show compassion unto us.[^2222]

*Canon 2. Ode 8. Heirmos:* The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its operation by the command of God, scorching the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^2223]

*Troparion 1:* By Thy descent Thou hast shown us the death of our enemies, as Thou art immortal; and by Thy divine power Thou hast revealed the entrances of life, O Immortal One, which Thy martyrs have now worthily obtained.[^2224]

*Troparion 2:* Vouchsafe the departed to delight in Thy noetic beauty, cleansing the shame of hideous sin, as Thou art the Lover of mankind; for Thou alone, O Master, wast shown to be a stranger to sin.[^2225]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou hast raised us up, O Christ, who had fallen into mortal dust, by Thy death granting life, and endless delight, and everlasting joy: whereof vouchsafe now the departed, as Thou art merciful.[^2226]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Great and awesome is the mystery of thy childbearing, O Mother of God: for thou didst give birth unto God, Whom death could not retain, and Whom the tomb could not corrupt. Therefore, O Most Pure Lady, all nations glorify thee.[^2227]

*Canon 1. Ode 9. Heirmos:* Her who supernaturally conceived in her womb in the flesh the Word Who before all ages shone forth from the Father, let us the faithful magnify with unceasing hymns.[^2228]

*For the Martyrs:* Shown to be steadfast against passions, mighty against the enemies, the passion-bearers...[^2229]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*For the Martyrs (continued):* ...took the victory, having suffered lawfully, and were crowned by God.[^2230]

*For the Hierarchs:* As sacred ministers of God, and imitators of the Good Shepherd, ye piously tended His sheep, O right-glorious high priests.[^2231]

*For the Ascetics & Holy Women:* With the venerable fasters, and sacred prophets, let us honor the multitude of women who suffered, and through fasting cast down the enemy.[^2232]

*Glory... For the Departed:*
The all-glorious multitude of Thy saints, O Christ, unceasingly prays Thee: show those whom Thou hast translated in faith, O Lord, to be partakers of eternal life.[^2233]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bear in the flesh the God of tender compassions, with the saints ever pray Him to save us from tribulations, O pure Mother and Virgin.[^2234]

*Canon 2. Ode 9. Heirmos:* Her who ineffably incarnated from virgin loins God Who shone forth as a luminary before the sun and came to us in the flesh: O blessed and all-pure Theotokos, thee do we magnify.[^2235]

*Troparion 1:* Having authority over both the living and the dead, O Author of life, grant unto those who have come from the earth unto Thee the heavenly inheritance, O Master, and the brightness of Thy saints and all-glorious passion-bearers.[^2236]

*Troparion 2:* Being the all-originating Word, Who of old gavest me being, and again bestowest well-being: in the longing-desired bosom of Abraham our forefather, settle Thy departed servants, as Thou art merciful.[^2237]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou art wholly light-bearing sweetness, O my Savior, Thou art wholly insatiable desire: with the torrents of Thy delight and the water of remission water the departed who unceasingly glorify Thee.[^2238]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Thee, O Theotokos, do we the faithful worthily bless, following thine inspired words; for thou alone among earthborn mortals didst bear God, Who destroyed the power of death, O Virgin Mother.[^2239]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs. (If Alleluia is appointed.) Tone 2:*
Having suffered for Christ even unto death, O passion-bearing martyrs, your souls are in the heavens in the hand of God, and throughout the whole world...[^2240]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs (continued):* ...your relics are venerated: hierarchs and tsars bow down, and all people rejoicing cry aloud with one accord: Precious before the Lord is the death of His saints.[^2241]

Every city and country honors your relics, O passion-bearing martyrs! For having suffered lawfully, ye received heavenly crowns; and for this cause ye are the boast of hierarchs, victory for tsars, and the beauty of churches.[^2242]

Having taken up the Cross of Christ as an invincible weapon, the holy martyrs abolished all the power of the devil and received heavenly crowns; they have become a wall for us, ever making intercession on our behalf.[^2243]

*For the Departed:*
Woe is me! What a struggle hath the soul when parting from the body! Woe is me! How many tears she then sheds, and there is none that pities her! Lifting up her eyes to the angels, she prays in vain; stretching out her hands to men, she hath none to help. Therefore, my beloved brethren, understanding the shortness of our life, let us ask of Christ rest for the departed, and for our souls great mercy.[^2244]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
Come, let us all glorify with unceasing hymns the Mother of Light; for she gave birth unto our salvation. And let us bring unto her 'Rejoice!', as unto her who alone gave birth unto the primordial God before all ages. Rejoice, O thou who didst fashion anew Eve our foremother! Rejoice, O all-pure Virgin unwedded![^2245]

*At the Aposticha, if Alleluia is not chanted, we sing Stichera for the Departed, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
The assault of death and corruption hast Thou cast down by Thy life-bearing death, O Master! Unto all hast Thou poured forth eternal life, and unto the dead of the earth Thou hast granted resurrection. Therefore we pray Thee, O Savior: give rest unto those who in faith have passed over unto Thee, and vouchsafe them Thy incorruptible glory, O Lover of mankind.[^2246]

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.[^2247]

That Thou mightest make men partakers of Thy divine Kingdom, O Christ, Thou didst endure crucifixion, and accepted voluntary death. For this cause we pray: by Thy tender mercy show forth those who in faith have passed over unto Thee as partakers of...[^2248]

*On Saturday at Matins, Tone 2.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed (continued):* ...Thy Kingdom, and vouchsafe them Thy sweet beauty, O Lover of mankind.[^2249]

*Verse:* Their souls shall dwell amid good things.[^2250]

Desiring to save Thine own creation, Thou didst with joy accomplish the truly awesome mystery of dispensation, as Thou art supremely good; and at a price Thou didst redeem the whole world by Thy precious Blood. Therefore we pray: vouchsafe deliverance with all the saints unto those who in faith have passed over unto Thee.[^2251]

*Verse:* And their remembrance is from generation to generation.[^2252]

Standing before Thine awesome, dreadful, and fearful throne, O Christ, they that have died from the beginning of the world await Thy righteous sentence and receive Thy divine justice. At that time give rest, O Savior, unto Thy servants who in faith have passed over unto Thee, where the choirs of the saints abide, and where joy is ineffable.[^2253]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
When my soul is about to be separated from life and the bodily bond, then stand by me, O Lady, and destroy the counsels of the incorporeal enemies, and crush the jaws of those who seek to devour me mercilessly; that unhindered I may pass by the princes of darkness standing in the air, O Bride of God![^2254]

*Then: It is good to give praise to the Lord (Psalm 91:2), and the rest. After this, the First Hour, and the Dismissal.*[^2255]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes, Tone 2:*
We bring unto Thee the cry of the thief, and we pray: Remember us, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom.[^2256]

Having been conformed unto the sufferings of Christ, O martyrs, ye ever heal the diverse passions of men.[^2257]

With the Prophets the Apostles, and with the Venerables the Teachers, well pleased the Creator of all.[^2258]

With all Thy saints, O Lord Who lovest mankind, establish all Thy servants who have passed away in faith, we pray Thee.[^2259]

*Glory... Trinity:*
O consubstantial Trinity, have compassion upon those who honor Thee, ever delivering all from the enemy's delusion and snares.[^2260]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Despise not the supplications of thy servants, O All-Blameless Lady, saving us from all tribulations and sorrows.[^2261]

*End of Tone 2.*[^2262]

---

### Notes

[^1328]: Physical Leaf p134 (Printed Folio 125 verso, unnumbered). Saturday Evening, Little Vespers (*Въ Сꙋббѡ́тꙋ въ ве́черъ, на ма́лѣй Вече́рни*). First Resurrection Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 2 (*Пре́жде вѣ̑къ ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ ро́ждшемꙋсѧ*).
[^1329]: Physical Leaf p134 (Printed Folio 125 verso). Saturday Little Vespers, Second Resurrection Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 2, referencing Colossians 2:14.
[^1330]: Physical Leaf p134 (Printed Folio 125 verso). Saturday Little Vespers, Third Resurrection Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 2.
[^1331]: Physical Leaf p134 (Printed Folio 125 verso). Saturday Little Vespers, Theotokion Dogmatikon in Tone 2 (*Ѽ преве́лїѧ та́инства!*), alluding to the closed gate of Ezekiel 44:1-2.
[^1332]: Physical Leaf p134 (Printed Folio 125 verso). Rubric for the Evening Hymn "O Gladsome Radiance" (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*) and the Saturday Evening Prokeimenon from Psalm 92:1 [LXX 92:1].
[^1333]: Physical Leaf p134 (Printed Folio 125 verso). Rubric for the prayer "Vouchsafe, O Lord" (*Сподо́би Гдⷭ҇и*) and the immediate singing of the Aposticha without intervening litanies at Little Vespers.
[^1334]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Saturday Little Vespers, Resurrection Sticheron at the Aposticha (*На стїхо́внѣ*) in Tone 2.
[^1335]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Psalm Stichos from Psalm 44:18 [LXX 44:18].
[^1336]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Saturday Little Vespers Aposticha, First Sticheron to the Theotokos, sung to the prosomoion model *Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва тѧ̀)*.
[^1337]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Psalm Stichos from Psalm 44:11 [LXX 44:11].
[^1338]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Saturday Little Vespers Aposticha, Second Sticheron to the Theotokos in Tone 2.
[^1339]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Psalm Stichos from Psalm 44:13 [LXX 44:13].
[^1340]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Saturday Little Vespers Aposticha, Third Sticheron to the Theotokos in Tone 2.
[^1341]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Saturday Little Vespers Aposticha, Doxasticon Theotokion in Tone 2 (*Кто́ тѧ по достоѧ́нїю похва́литъ*).
[^1342]: Physical Leaf p135 (Printed Folio 126, рл҃г). Concluding rubrics of Little Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29-32), Trisagion prayers, Sunday Resurrection Troparion in Tone 2 (*Є҆гда̀ снизше́лъ є҆сѝ*), Theotokion (*Всѧ̑ па́че смы́сла*), and the Dismissal.
[^1343]: Physical Leaf p136 (Printed Folio 126 verso, unnumbered). Saturday at Great Vespers (*Въ Сꙋббо́тꙋ на вели́цѣй вече́рни*). Opening rubrics for setting 10 verses on "Lord, I have cried" (*поста́вимъ стїхѡ́въ ῖ*), distributing 3 Octoechos Resurrection stichera, 4 Anatolian (Eastern) stichera, and 3 of the Menaion.
[^1344]: Physical Leaf p136 (Printed Folio 126 verso). Saturday Great Vespers, First Resurrection Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 2 (*Пре́жде вѣ̑къ ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ ро́ждшемꙋсѧ*).
[^1345]: Physical Leaf p136 (Printed Folio 126 verso). Saturday Great Vespers, Second Resurrection Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 2, referencing Colossians 2:14.
[^1346]: Physical Leaf p136 (Printed Folio 126 verso). Saturday Great Vespers, Third Resurrection Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 2.

[^1669]: Theotokion of Ode 5: Perpetual virginity of the Mother of God (*aeiparthenia*) and her maternal grief at the Crucifixion (John 19:25–27).
[^1670]: Ode 5 heirmos of the second canon (to the Theotokos), Tone 2, invoking the prophetic prayer of Isaiah 26:9: "With my soul have I desired Thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I seek Thee early: for when Thy judgments are in the earth, the inhabitants of the world will learn righteousness."
[^1671]: Affirmation of the orthodox Christological dogma defined at the Fourth Ecumenical Council (Chalcedon, 451), confessing Christ as true God and true man, born without human seed of the Virgin Mary.
[^1672]: Ode 6 heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 2, drawing upon Jonah 2:2, 9: "Out of the belly of hell cried I, and Thou heardest my voice... I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of thanksgiving."
[^1673]: Allusion to Genesis 48:13–14, wherein the patriarch Jacob laid his hands crosswise upon the heads of Joseph's sons, Ephraim and Manasseh, historically interpreted by the Church Fathers as an Old Testament prefiguration of the Cross of Christ.
[^1674]: Allusion to the Pauline theology of the abolition of reigning sin and death through the voluntary crucifixion of Christ (cf. Romans 5:21; 6:12).
[^1675]: Martyrikon troparion commemorating the sacred wounds of the martyrs as heavenly ornaments and trophies of victory.
[^1676]: Typological imagery drawn from the Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25:1–13), wherein the martyrs maintain their lamps unquenched by fueling them with their own blood, gaining joyful entry into the celestial bridal chamber.
[^1677]: Stavrotheotokion praising the Mother of God whose Son vanquished the adversary upon the Tree of the Cross.
[^1678]: Penitential Ode 6 heirmos of the second canon, invoking Psalm 129:1 [LXX 129:1 / MT 130:1]: "Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord."
[^1679]: Reflection upon the voluntary kenosis of the Word of God, who willed to dwell in the womb of the Virgin to bestow incorruption upon mortal mankind.
[^1680]: Marian dogmatic title *Platytera ton ouranon* ("More spacious than the heavens"), exalting the Theotokos for containing within her womb the uncontainable Word of God.
[^1681]: Ode 7 heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 2, commemorating the Three Holy Children preserved in the furnace of Babylon (Daniel 3), with the fire transformed into spiritual dew through the pre-incarnate presence of Christ.
[^1682]: Reference to the fiery sword that turned every way to keep the way of the tree of life (Genesis 3:24), which yields and grants passage to mankind once Christ's side is pierced by the soldier's spear upon the Cross (John 19:34).
[^1683]: Typological interpretation of the bronze serpent set up upon a pole by Moses in the wilderness (Numbers 21:8–9), fulfilled in the lifting up of the Son of Man upon the Cross (John 3:14–15).
[^1684]: The martyrs exalted as sons and brethren of Christ and joint-heirs of His heavenly kingdom, having shared in His suffering and death (Romans 8:17; Hebrews 2:11–12).
[^1685]: Vivid hymnographic enumeration of the varied bodily torments endured by the early Christian martyrs (crucifixion, piercing with lances, sword-strokes, immersion in fire and water, wheel-breaking).
[^1686]: Traditional patristic and hymnographic metaphor of Christ as the mystical Cluster of Grapes (*Botrys*) suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, whose crushed juice yields the life-giving wine that dispels the intoxication of carnal passions.
[^1687]: The foundational Byzantine soteriological principle of *theosis* (deification): God became man from the Virgin's pure blood in order that man might be made partaker of the divine nature.
[^1688]: Typological parallel between the Babylonian furnace, which did not burn the Three Youths, and the virginal womb of Mary, which was unconsumed by the divine fire of the Godhead.
[^1689]: Ode 8 heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 2, based on the Song of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:57).
[^1690]: Typological reflection upon the life-giving stream of blood and water flowing from the side of the crucified Savior (John 19:34), drying up the rivers of pagan polytheism and watering the Church with true piety.
[^1691]: The cosmic convulsions accompanying the Crucifixion of Christ recorded in Matthew 27:45, 51–52: the solar eclipse, the rending of the temple veil, the splitting of rocks, and the harrowing of hell.
[^1692]: Allusion to Psalm 67:36 [LXX 67:36 / MT 68:35]: "Marvelous is God in His saints," affirming the miraculous curative power resident in the relics of the holy martyrs.
[^1693]: Acclamation of the heroic endurance of the passion-bearers amidst wild beasts, foul stables, extreme cold, scorching heat, and torture.
[^1694]: Allusion to Galatians 3:13: "Christ hath redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us."
[^1695]: Ode 8 heirmos of the second canon, praising the Lord who preserved the Hebrew youths in the furnace.
[^1696]: Marian invocation uniting the title of the Theotokos as the immaculate Ewe-lamb (*Amnas*) with Christ as the Lamb of God (*Amnos tou Theou*, John 1:29).
[^1697]: Allusion to 1 Thessalonians 5:5: "Ye are all the children of light, and the children of the day."
[^1698]: Allusion to Christ as the fountain of Living Water (John 4:10; 7:37–38) brought forth through the virginal conception.
[^1699]: Ode 9 heirmos of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 2, drawing upon Luke 1:78–79 (the Dayspring from on high visiting those in darkness) and John 11:52 (gathering together into one the scattered children of God).
[^1700]: Allusion to the suffering servant prophecy of Isaiah 53:5: "He was wounded for our transgressions... and with His stripes we are healed."
[^1701]: Ancient Christian tradition based on Isaiah 60:13 concerning the three species of wood comprising the Holy Cross: cypress, pine, and cedar, symbolizing the incorruptibility, fragrance, and majesty of Christ's sacrifice, combined with the Chalcedonian confession of one hypostasis in two natures.
[^1702]: Patristic imagery of Satan vanquished and made a laughingstock like a captured sparrow, drawn from Job 40:24 [LXX] / 41:5: "Wilt thou play with him as with a bird?"
[^1703]: Affirmation of the double destiny of the martyrs: their earthly blood hallows the soil, while their sanctified spirits join the angelic hierarchies before the divine throne.
[^1704]: Stavrotheotokion of Ode 9, lamenting the paradox of the Creator of all nature hanging upon the wood of the Cross.
[^1705]: Ode 9 heirmos of the second canon (to the Theotokos), Tone 2, celebrating the renewal of fallen Adam through the Incarnation of God the Word.
[^1706]: Classical Byzantine catalog of Marian epithets: steadfast protectress (*prostates*), expectation (*elpis*), wall (*teichos*), firm shelter (*skepē*), and unshakable harbor (*limēn*).
[^1707]: Church Slavonic `чꙋжда́гѡ` corresponds to the Greek `τοῦ ἀλλοτρίου` ("the stranger" / "the alien"), referring to the devil and demonic thoughts invading the human soul.
[^1708]: Allusion to Hebrews 2:14, affirming that through His death on the Cross Christ destroyed him that had the power of death, that is, the devil.
[^1709]: Psalm 89:14, 16 [LXX 89:14, 16 / MT 90:14, 16], serving as the first Psalm stichos for the daily Matins aposticha.
[^1710]: Allusion to 2 Corinthians 8:9: "For ye know the grace of our Lord Jesus Christ, that, though He was rich, yet for your sakes He became poor, that ye through His poverty might be rich."
[^1711]: Psalm 89:17 [LXX 89:17 / MT 90:17], serving as the second Psalm stichos for the daily Matins aposticha.
[^1712]: Martyrikon sticheron extolling the universal honor accorded to martyrs' relics, citing their lawful struggle (2 Timothy 2:5).
[^1713]: Concluding lines of the Tone 2 Stavrotheotokion prosomoion to the model "When from the Tree" (*Egda ot dreva*), continued from physical leaf p205.
[^1714]: Opening troparion of the Beatitudes at Divine Liturgy on Wednesday, recalling the penitent thief upon the cross (Luke 23:42).
[^1715]: Allusion to John 12:32: "And I, if I be lifted up from the earth, will draw all men unto Myself."
[^1716]: Theological reflection on Colossians 2:14: "Blotting out the handwriting of ordinances that was against us, which was contrary to us, and took it out of the way, nailing it to His cross."
[^1717]: The martyrs glorified as partakers of the sufferings and subsequent eternal glory of Christ (Romans 8:17; 1 Peter 4:13).
[^1718]: Trinitarian doxological troparion of the Beatitudes, confessing the consubstantiality and co-eternity of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.
[^1719]: Stavrotheotokion concluding the Beatitudes on Wednesday.
[^1720]: Canonical H2 day opening for Thursday in the Octoechos cycle, commemorating the Twelve Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia.
[^1721]: Typological allegory identifying the Apostles with the four mystical rivers of Eden (Genesis 2:10), whose preaching irrigated the whole earth and harvested souls for the kingdom of God.
[^1722]: The Apostles characterized as radiant rays and eyewitnesses (*autoptai*, Luke 1:2) of Christ, the true Sun of Righteousness and Dayspring from on high (Luke 1:78; Malachi 4:2).
[^1723]: Apostolic typology contrasting the stone tablets of the Mosaic Law with the living tablets of the new covenant in the Holy Spirit (2 Corinthians 3:3; Luke 11:20).
[^1724]: Celebrated hymnographic wordplay between the city of Myra in Lycia (where Saint Nicholas presided as archbishop) and the sweet-smelling spiritual myrrh (*myron*) of his virtues and post-mortem wonderworking relics.
[^1725]: Supplication unto Saint Nicholas as the swift deliverer of those tempest-tossed upon the sea of life and beset by sudden tribulations.
[^1726]: Applying the Dominical title "light of the world" (Matthew 5:14) to Saint Nicholas, whose radiant life illumines souls darkened by sinful passions.
[^1727]: Apostolic Theotokion synthesizing the Incarnation of the Word with the worldwide missionary commissioning of the Apostles.
[^1728]: Allusion to Acts 5:15, recording that the inhabitants of Jerusalem brought forth the sick into the streets, that at least the shadow of Peter passing by might overshadow them.
[^1729]: Psalm 122:1–2 [LXX 122:1–2 / MT 123:1–2], the first Psalm verse appointed for the daily Vespers aposticha.
[^1730]: Allusion to the cry of the terrified disciples during the tempest on the Sea of Galilee (Matthew 8:25): "Lord, save us: we perish!"
[^1731]: Psalm 122:3–4 [LXX 122:3–4 / MT 123:3–4], the second Psalm verse appointed for the daily Vespers aposticha.
[^1732]: Martyrikon apostichon praising the holy martyrs as heavenly physicians capable of healing bodily and spiritual afflictions after their earthly departure.
[^1733]: Metaphor of the Mother of God as the fruitful olive tree in the house of God (Psalm 51:10 [LXX 51:10 / MT 52:8]).
[^1734]: Ode 1 heirmos of the Supplicatory Canon to the Theotokos at Small Compline on Wednesday evening, based on the Exodus canticle of Moses (Exodus 15:1–2).
[^1735]: Canonical hymnographic refrain addressed directly to the Mother of God throughout the Compline canon.
[^1736]: Ode 3 heirmos of the Compline canon, invoking the tree of the Cross which abolished sin.
[^1737]: Church Slavonic `благопремѣ́нна` translates the Greek liturgical term `εὐδιάллаκτον` ("easily appeased," "reconciled," "propitiated"), entreating the Theotokos to make God merciful to penitent sinners.
[^1738]: Christ addressed as the Hypostatic Wisdom of God (1 Corinthians 1:24) carried in the arms of the Theotokos.
[^1739]: Ode 4 heirmos of the Compline canon, derived from the canticle of the Prophet Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3:2).
[^1740]: Ode 5 heirmos of the Compline canon, based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^1741]: Allusion to Romans 10:4: "For Christ is the end of the law for righteousness to every one that believs."
[^1742]: Conclusion of the supplication of Ode 5, continued from physical leaf p209.
[^1743]: Penitential Ode 6 heirmos of the Compline canon, based on Psalm 129:1 [LXX 129:1 / MT 130:1].
[^1744]: Allusion to Isaiah 61:10 and the spiritual robe of righteousness defiled by sin and restored to joy.
[^1745]: Allusion to Ephesians 1:7 and Revelation 1:5: Christ who washed us from our sins in His own blood.
[^1746]: Sessional Hymn (Sessional Hymn) of the Compline canon, set to the melodic model "We have become partakers of the divine" (Greek: Θείας γεγόναμεν / Church Slavonic: Бж҃твеннагѡ бы́хомъ), an incipit drawn from 2 Peter 1:4 ("that by these ye might be partakers of the divine nature").
[^1747]: Ode 7 heirmos of the Compline canon, commemorating the Three Youths who refused to bow down to the golden idol erected by Nebuchadnezzar on the plain of Dura (Daniel 3:1–18).
[^1748]: Allusion to Colossians 2:14 on Christ tearing asunder the handwritten record of our debt upon the Cross.
[^1749]: Allusion to the lamentation of Psalm 68:21 [LXX 68:21 / MT 69:20]: "I looked for some to take pity, but there was none; and for comforters, but I found none."
[^1750]: Patristic imagery of the white baptismal robe (*chiton*) tarnished through negligent sin and washed clean through repentance and Marian intercession.
[^1751]: Opening words of the Ode 8 heirmos of the Compline canon, catching at the bottom of physical leaf p210 and continued on physical leaf p211.
[^1752]: Ode 8 heirmos conclusion of the Supplicatory Canon to the Theotokos at Small Compline, Tone 2, drawing upon the Song of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:57).
[^1753]: Troparion of Ode 8, pleading for freedom from passions (*apatheia*) and divine illumination amidst the assault of vices.
[^1754]: Troparion of Ode 8, praising the Mother of God as a tower of strength (allusion to Proverbs 18:10), ladder/step (*bathron* / *stepen'*), and steadfast protectress of the faithful.
[^1755]: Doxasticon of Ode 8, confessing the Word of the immortal Father born of the Virgin Mary, delivering from death all who praise Him.
[^1756]: Theotokion of Ode 8, invoking the Virgin Mary as the ungrudging fountain of healings for believers.
[^1757]: Ode 9 heirmos of the Compline canon, derived from Luke 1:78–79 and celebrating the Incarnation of God the Word to renew fallen Adam.
[^1758]: Troparion of Ode 9, addressing the Theotokos as the Mother of the true Life (John 14:6) and conduit of eternal celestial food.
[^1759]: Troparion of Ode 9, invoking the Virgin as the Gate of divine light (*porta lucis*), dispelling demonic gloom by her immaterial radiance.
[^1760]: Doxasticon of Ode 9, entreating the Mother of God to heal spiritual and physical sicknesses and loosen the bonds of sorrow.
[^1761]: Concluding Theotokion of the Compline canon, confessing the Chalcedonian dogma of the pre-eternal Son born of the Father becoming perfect man in the womb of the Virgin.
[^1762]: Rubrical directive concluding Small Compline with the Marian megalynarion *Axion Estin* ("It is truly meet"), the Trisagion prayers, Our Father, troparia, and the standard daily dismissal.
[^1763]: Opening of Thursday Matins in the weekly Octoechos cycle, dedicated to the memory of the Twelve Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia.
[^1764]: First Apostolic Sessional Hymn after the first stichology of the Psalter, celebrating Christ who made unlearned fishermen wiser than worldly rhetors (1 Corinthians 1:26–29) and sent them forth to preach the Gospel.
[^1765]: Psalm 18:5 [LXX 18:5 / MT 19:4], the standard apostolic verse applied by Saint Paul to the worldwide dissemination of the Gospel (Romans 10:18).
[^1766]: Second Apostolic Sessional Hymn after the first stichology, commemorating the disciples whose simple proclamation of the Cross confounded worldly philosophy.
[^1767]: Theotokion after the first stichology, addressing the Virgin Mary as the cloud of the unsetting Light (allusion to Isaiah 19:1).
[^1768]: First Apostolic Sessional Hymn after the second stichology, depicting the Apostles as fishers of men (Matthew 4:19) who drew all nations into the net of the Holy Spirit.
[^1769]: Psalm 88:6 [LXX 88:6 / MT 89:5]: "The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord," appointed as the first psalm verse of the second stichology.
[^1770]: Psalm 67:36 [LXX 67:36 / MT 68:35]: "Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel," introducing the Martyrikon troparion.
[^1771]: Martyrikon troparion after the second stichology, praising the martyrs whose blood sanctified the earth and whose relics sanctify churches.
[^1772]: Theotokion after the second stichology, confessing the deification (*theosis*) of human nature made partakers of the divine nature (2 Peter 1:4) through Christ's Incarnation.
[^1773]: Melodic model (*Podoben*) heading: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (*Miloserdïia sushchi istochnik* / Greek: Εὐσπλαγχνίας ὑπάρχουσα πηγή), an automelon of Tone 2.
[^1774]: Third Kathisma Sessional Hymn to the Apostles, invoking the Disciples as radiant luminaries enlightening the world under the sun (Philippians 2:15).
[^1775]: Sessional Hymn to Saint Nicholas, celebrating his miracles that dispel the gloom of tribulations.
[^1776]: Theotokion after the third stichology, imploring the Mother of God together with the Apostles to obtain forgiveness of sins and amendment of life.
[^1777]: The first Canon of Thursday Matins, composed by Saint Theophanes the Branded (Graptos, c. 778–845), dedicated to the Holy Apostles.
[^1778]: Ode 1 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, drawing upon the Song of the Sea (Exodus 15:1–2) and the overthrow of Pharaoh's army in the Red Sea.
[^1779]: Troparion of Ode 1, portraying the Apostles as divine lightning-flashes (*fulgura divina*) dispelling the darkness of sensual pleasures.
[^1780]: Troparion of Ode 1, invoking the Apostles as friends of Christ (John 15:14–15) to rescue the soul that has befriended the devil through sinful deeds.
[^1781]: Troparion of Ode 1, comparing the deadened soul to Lazarus who lay four days in the tomb (John 11:38–44), praying for resurrection through apostolic prayers.
[^1782]: Theotokion of Ode 1, invoking the Virgin Mary who gave birth to the Good God, together with the Prophets, Martyrs, and Apostles.
[^1783]: The second Canon of Thursday Matins, composed by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886), bearing an acrostic in the original Greek: "Accept a loving prayer, O Nicholas" (*Mōlbū liubēznū Nīkōlaē prīīmī* / Greek: Δεχου ποθητην, Νικολαε, προσφοραν).
[^1784]: Ode 1 heirmos of the Canon to Saint Nicholas, based on Exodus 15. The 1895 Lviv print exhibits an epigraphic haplography (*razdiel'shemu liudi* instead of *razdiel'shemu morie, i nastavľshemu liudi*), here faithfully translated as inked and noted.
[^1785]: Troparion of Ode 1 (St. Nicholas), describing the hierarch standing before the heavenly throne of grace (Hebrews 4:16) to obtain mercy for his flock.
[^1786]: Troparion of Ode 1 (St. Nicholas), exalting the saint as an earthly protector guarding believers day and night against demonic temptations.
[^1787]: Troparion of Ode 1 (St. Nicholas), employing the metaphor of Saint Nicholas as the daystar / morning star (*phosphoros*, 2 Peter 1:19), whose radiant beams dispel sinful darkness.
[^1788]: Theotokion of Ode 1 (Canon 2), supplicating the Mother of God to heal bodily sicknesses and spiritual wounds, delivering the soul from eternal fire.
[^1789]: Ode 3 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, based on 1 Kingdoms 2:1–2 [1 Samuel 2:1–2] and Isaiah 35:1 ("the desert shall blossom as the lily").
[^1790]: Troparion of Ode 3, commemorating the unlettered Apostles who, instructed by the Father's Wisdom in the Holy Spirit, confounded Greek philosophy (1 Corinthians 1:20–21).
[^1791]: Troparion of Ode 3, praying that the spiritual barrenness of the soul may be transformed into the fruitfulness of virtuous deeds.
[^1792]: Troparion of Ode 3, beseeching Christ the Benefactor to heal the venomous bite of the serpent through the prayers of the Apostles.
[^1793]: Theotokion of Ode 3 (Apostles), entreating the Virgin Mary together with all the Apostles for deliverance from harm, adversity, and perils.
[^1794]: Ode 3 heirmos of the Canon to Saint Nicholas, celebrating Christ who mortified sin upon the Tree of the Cross and established believers in His fear.
[^1795]: Troparion of Ode 3 (St. Nicholas), addressing Christ as the divine Fountain of healings, preserving His servant unharmed.
[^1796]: Troparion of Ode 3 (St. Nicholas), entreating Saint Nicholas to restore the defeated mind and protect it against visible and invisible adversaries.
[^1797]: Opening of Troparion 3 of Ode 3 (St. Nicholas), confessing Christ the sole Good One who bestowed Saint Nicholas as a benevolent protector of mankind, continued on leaf p215.
[^1798]: Conclusion of Troparion 3 of Ode 3 (St. Nicholas), continued from leaf p214.
[^1799]: Theotokion of Ode 3 (Canon 2), praising the All-Spotless Virgin as strength, joy, gladness, and a firm wall of defense.
[^1800]: Ode 4 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, derived from the Canticle of Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3:2–3): the Incarnate Lord coming not as an ambassador or an angel, but Himself in the flesh to save mankind.
[^1801]: Troparion of Ode 4, crying out for the spiritual food of salvation to nourish the soul starving amidst passions.
[^1802]: Troparion of Ode 4, typological imagery based on Habakkuk 3:8, 15: the Apostles as the horses of the Lord riding upon the sea of the world, troubling the bitter salt waters of unbelief.
[^1803]: Troparion of Ode 4, praising the Apostles who heralded Christ the Sun of Righteousness unto those in darkness, entreating them to enlighten the sinner and restrain evil thoughts.
[^1804]: Theotokion of Ode 4 (Apostles), asking the All-Hymned Mother of God to join the Apostles in intercession for deliverance from sins and afflictions.
[^1805]: Ode 4 heirmos of the Canon to Saint Nicholas, also drawing upon Habakkuk 3:2: hearing the Lord's report and being struck with awe at His condescension (*synkatabasis*) to seek the lost sheep.
[^1806]: Troparion of Ode 4 (St. Nicholas), venerating Saint Nicholas for adorning the episcopal throne of Myra with virtues, entreated to adorn the uncomely soul.
[^1807]: Troparion of Ode 4 (St. Nicholas), entreating the saint to smooth the path to heaven, calming worldly billows and guiding the soul to the harbor of eternal life.
[^1808]: Troparion of Ode 4 (St. Nicholas), invoking the hierarch as a hearer of divine words to deliver the supplicant from demonic snares and lawless men.
[^1809]: Opening of Theotokion of Ode 4 (Canon 2), supplicating the Mother of God for protection day and night against demonic assaults, continued on leaf p216.
[^1810]: Ode 5 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, invoking 1 Timothy 2:5 ("one Mediator between God and men, the man Christ Jesus") and the transition from the night of ignorance to the light of the Father.
[^1811]: Troparion of Ode 5, depicting the Great Shepherd (Hebrews 13:20) sending His disciples as sheep amidst wolves (Matthew 10:16), transforming them by the power of holy baptism.
[^1812]: Troparion of Ode 5, the Apostles celebrated for enlightening hearts in the darkness of delusion with the divine light of the Gospel.
[^1813]: Troparion of Ode 5, penitential plea to Christ before the end, crying out for pardon for the sake of His Apostles.
[^1814]: Theotokion of Ode 5 (Apostles), the Theotokos addressed as the dwelling-place of Light, invoked alongside the Apostles for deliverance from distress.
[^1815]: Ode 5 heirmos of the Canon to Saint Nicholas, based on Isaiah 26:9: seeking God early and walking in the light of His commandments.
[^1816]: Troparion of Ode 5 (St. Nicholas), Saint Nicholas venerated as a faithful fulfiller of the Divine Law, asked to help believers keep the commandments and escape demonic harm.
[^1817]: Troparion of Ode 5 (St. Nicholas), alluding to the celebrated miracle of Saint Nicholas appearing in dreams to the Emperor Constantine and the prefect Ablabius to deliver three innocent Roman military officers (*stratēgai*) condemned to death.
[^1818]: Troparion of Ode 5 (St. Nicholas), Saint Nicholas invoked as Christ's sacred minister and guarantor/surety (*engyetēs*) for sinners before the judgment seat of God.
[^1819]: Theotokion of Ode 5 (Canon 2), the Virgin Mary addressed as the Mother of the Lord (*Kyriotokos* / *Gospodoroditelnitsa*), delivering the soul from violent passions.
[^1820]: Ode 6 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, invoking the prayer of Jonah 2:2–3 from the belly of the whale and Psalm 129:1.
[^1821]: Troparion of Ode 6, the Apostles depicted as bearers of the Living Water (John 4:10, 7:38) to quench the soul parched by the scorching heat of sin.
[^1822]: Troparion of Ode 6 (Apostles), the Apostles celebrated as noetic heavens declaring the ineffable glory of God (Psalm 18:2 [LXX 18:2 / MT 19:1]).
[^1823]: Troparion of Ode 6 (Apostles), the sinner tempest-tossed upon the surging deep of life fleeing unto Christ the divine Helmsman (*Kybernētēs*).

[^1826]: Troparion of Ode 6 (St. Nicholas), playing upon the name of Myra (*Myra* / *myron*), Saint Nicholas as primate perfuming the assembly of believers with sweet virtues and dispelling the stench of sin.
[^1827]: Troparion of Ode 6 (St. Nicholas), the heart of Saint Nicholas praised as shining brighter than the sun, dispersing the gloom of trials.
[^1828]: Troparion of Ode 6 (St. Nicholas), Saint Nicholas possessing the broad expanse of mercy, strengthening the sinner to tread the narrow way (Matthew 7:14) unto the Lord.
[^1829]: Theotokion of Ode 6 (Canon 2), constant recourse unto the Mother of God as a swift helper rescuing from grievous torment.
[^1830]: Ode 7 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, commemorating the Three Youths in the furnace of Babylon, upon whom Christ spread a spiritual dew against the tyrant's decree.
[^1831]: Troparion of Ode 7, the Apostles ignited with the fire of the Holy Spirit at Pentecost (Acts 2:3–4), extinguishing the coals of demonic deception.
[^1832]: Troparion of Ode 7, the Apostles renouncing the world and worldly lusts (1 John 2:15–17) out of love for Christ who united Himself to mortal flesh.
[^1833]: Troparion of Ode 7, penitential supplication unto Christ the righteous Judge and Knower of hearts (Acts 1:24) not to condemn the sinner into eternal fire.

[^1836]: Ode 7 heirmos of the Canon to Saint Nicholas, celebrating the Three Hebrew Children refusing to worship the golden image on the plain of Dura (Daniel 3).

[^1839]: Troparion of Ode 7 (St. Nicholas), recalling Saint Nicholas appearing in a dream unto the Emperor to rescue innocent men appointed to die.
[^1840]: Theotokion of Ode 7 (Canon 2), inscribing the Pure Virgin as the sole guardian of one's whole life and intercessor for the world.
[^1841]: Ode 8 heirmos of the Apostolic Canon, the fiery furnace in Babylon separating its operation, burning the Chaldeans while bedewing the faithful Hebrew youths (Daniel 3).

[^1843]: Troparion of Ode 8, healing the unrectified heart tempest-tossed by passions, through the supplications of Christ's disciples.
[^1844]: Troparion of Ode 8, exhortation to the soul to weep bitterly before the arrival of uncomforted lamentation in the day of judgment.
[^1845]: Theotokion of Ode 8 (Apostles), the unburning furnace of Babylon prefiguring the virginal conception and childbearing of the Mother of God without corruption.
[^1846]: Ode 8 heirmos of the Canon to Saint Nicholas, the Song of the Three Holy Youths in the furnace (Daniel 3:57).
[^1847]: Troparion of Ode 8 (St. Nicholas), Saint Nicholas receiving from God the power to bind and loose (Matthew 16:19; 18:18), loosing the bonds of sins.
[^1848]: Troparion of Ode 8 (St. Nicholas), the divine visitation of Saint Nicholas smoothing the path of the humble soul day and night.
[^1849]: Troparion of Ode 8 (St. Nicholas), praying for Saint Nicholas to extend his helping hand and preserve the supplicant from bitter death, even as he once saved the condemned youths.

[^1854]: Troparion of Ode 9, the glorious and all-wise Apostles invoked to deliver the faithful from all harm, wrath, sin, and distress.

[^1863]: Theotokion of Ode 9 (Canon 2), pleading for the Lord's mercy at the Judgment, joined by the Virgin Mary, the apostolic choir, and Saint Nicholas.

[^1865]: Opening of the Matins Aposticha for Thursday, Tone 2, dedicated to the Holy Apostles.

[^1867]: Conclusion of the first Apostolic Apostichon from leaf p220, praising the unlettered fishermen who, though simple Jews, theologized the sublime mysteries of divine grace.
[^1868]: Psalm 89:14, 16 [LXX 89:14, 16 / MT 90:14, 16], appointed as the first psalm verse of the Aposticha at Thursday Matins.
